- **Encirclement campaigns and famine (1977–1979)** (`tl.conflicts.famine_1977_1979`, draft, 8 sources): Sensitive topic. The 84,200 CAVR figure is the minimum of excess deaths from hunger and illness for the whole period 1974–1999 (HRDAG warns it is often wrongly given for 1977–1979 only); 'most in the late 1970s' follows a CAVR statement that the overwhelming number occurred in 1977–78. Camp population: 300,000–370,000 by 1979 (Michigan War Studies Review) vs about 200,000 in ~150 resettlement areas (US cable, 1979) – both given as estimates. Matebian: 22 November 1978 per Wikipedia/Fundasaun Mahein (some sources say December 1978). 'Catholic sources' naming the campaign and 18 months: Wikipedia (East Timor genocide). The sentence on internal violence among FRETILIN leaders (1976–1978) is general knowledge from CAVR – check wording. Tetun terms to check: 'kampu tranzitu', 'kampu reinstalasaun', 'sirku no aniquilasaun', 'moras-na'in'.
- **The Kraras massacre (1983)** (`tl.conflicts.kraras_1983`, draft, 6 sources): Sensitive topic; numbers differ by source: Wikipedia (citing CAVR) 'more than 200', Wikipedia list of massacres 287 for Viqueque Aug–Oct 1983, church accounts 'more than 300'. The 8 August 1983 attack and '16 soldiers killed' come from a Jakarta Post opinion article (another account mentions one soldier); keep 'reports say'. Ceasefire meeting 23 March 1983 (Kammen, Cornell 'Indonesia' 87); talks collapsed in August 1983 (CIDOB). Possible links to named Indonesian officers are disputed and left out on purpose. Tetun to check: 'Vale Feto-Faluk sira', 'para-funu' (ceasefire), 'selu fali' (retaliate), 'Hansip/Ratih' explanations.
- **Santa Cruz massacre (12 November 1991)** (`tl.conflicts.santa_cruz_1991`, draft, 7 sources): Date of Sebastião Gomes's death differs: Wikipedia (Motael Church) says night of 27 October 1991, Portuguese/local sources say 28 October – written as 'night of 27 to 28 October'. Death toll: Amnesty 1991 said 50–100, ICJ 1992 50–200, Wikipedia at least 250 (250–271), Portuguese list 271 dead / 250 disappeared / 386 wounded – all given as estimates. Max Stahl died in Brisbane in 2021 (ABC/Catholic Leader; year not re-checked). 'Turning point' quote of Ali Alatas reported by ETAN/Wikipedia. Annual gathering at Motael and Santa Cruz is general knowledge. Tetun to check: 'kintál' (church grounds), 'faixa' (banner), 'pontu viragem', 'fatuk-rate' (gravestone).
- **The violence of 1999** (`tl.conflicts.violence_1999`, draft, 7 sources): Sensitive topic; numbers are estimates. Liquiçá (6 April 1999): sources give about 30 to about 200 dead; a UN-commissioned report says up to 60. Suai (6 September 1999): 26 bodies exhumed in West Timor (UN inquiry), 27 victims in a 2002 trial, up to 200 in other sources; the third priest's name differs between sources (Francisco Soares / Francisco Tavares dos Reis) so only two names are given. 1,400 civilians killed (Wikipedia); 'more than 300,000 displaced' (USAID 20 Sept 1999) – other sources say about 250,000 went to West Timor. Turnout 98.6% and 78.5% are the official results (general knowledge, not re-searched); January 1999 Habibie announcement and 15 September UNSC approval also general knowledge. 'Most of those tried in Jakarta were acquitted' – check wording. Tetun to check: 'besi mean-mutin', 'Timór Loro-Monu' (West Timor), 'absolvisaun', 'responsabilidade institusionál'.
- **The 2006 crisis** (`tl.conflicts.crisis_2006`, draft, 7 sources): Numbers differ between sources: petitioners 591–600 (UN: 594); deaths 'at least 25' to 'at least 37'; displaced 130,000–150,000. Verified: 24–28 April demonstrations (UN report), Alkatiri resigned 26 June 2006, Ramos-Horta appointed PM 10 July 2006, foreign forces from 26 May 2006 at the request to Australia, NZ, Malaysia and Portugal, more than 2,200 foreign peacekeepers. From general knowledge, not re-checked: the 25 May 2006 shooting of unarmed police under UN escort, the name 'International Stabilisation Force', UNMIT creation date 25 August 2006 (UNSC resolution 1704), 'most camps closed by 2009' and the petitioners' settlement (2008). The person-level responsibilities found by the UN Commission of Inquiry are left out on purpose. Tetun to check: 'petisionáriu', 'dezlokadu', 'hasai an' (resign), 'tiru malu'.
- **11 February 2008: attacks on the President and the Prime Minister** (`tl.conflicts.attacks_2008`, draft, 7 sources): Verified by search: 11 February 2008 attacks, President seriously wounded, Reinado and one of his men killed, PM's motorcade attacked without injuries, Salsinha and 11 rebels surrendered 29 April 2008 (Ermera), President discharged from hospital 19 March and treated about two months in Australia, 2010 court findings on who did not fire. From general knowledge, not re-checked: Reinado's arrest (July 2006), Becora escape (end of August 2006), Same attack (March 2007), 'returning from his morning walk', 'about an hour later', the type of emergency measures (state of siege/emergency, curfew) and the exact date of return (April 2008, often given as 17 April). Acting President: Vicente Guterres then Fernando 'La Sama' de Araújo (old Lafa data) – written only as 'leaders of the National Parliament'. Tetun to check: 'estadu exesaun', 'Prezidente interinu', 'knaar'.
- **Truth and reconciliation: CAVR, Chega! and the CTF** (`tl.conflicts.reconciliation`, draft, 10 sources): Statements: sources give 7,669 or 7,699 (written 'about 7,700'). Eight national public hearings and about 700 urgent reparations: ICTJ/UBC peace database; the hearing themes listed are from general knowledge – check. 'Most violations by Indonesian forces and auxiliaries; Timorese parties also responsible, especially 1974–1976' summarises Chega! from general knowledge – check wording against the report. Chega! to Parliament 28 November 2005 (ETAN/Hansard); to the UN Secretary-General January or February 2006 (sources differ, left out). CTF began 11 August 2005; report 15 July 2008 in Bali; first bilateral truth commission (Wikipedia). CNC created by decree-law in 2016 (PM Rui Maria de Araújo), operational by July 2017 (AJAR). June 2026 archive inauguration: from the title of a Government speech PDF dated 9 June 2026 – check details. Tetun: CAVR name follows Konstituisaun art. 162 ('Komisaun Simu, Lia-Loos no Rekonsiliasaun'); 'Komisaun Verdade no Amizade (KVA)' and 'Naton ona!' for 'Chega!' need a native check.

## State and politics (12)

- **The Constitution (Lei-Inan) of 2002** (`tl.politics.constitution`, draft, 7 sources): Based on the official Tetun text of the Constitution (local research copy; please add the online URL of the Tetun edition as a source). Check: 'about six months' of drafting (NIU/Wikipedia); Lao Hamutuk's English copy says 'final version approved 15 March 2002' while the preamble and Wikipedia give 22 March 2002; the statement that the text was never revised up to 2025 (no amendment found; revision debate in 2023–2025 per Tatoli and RTP). Wiki pt title 'Constituição de Timor-Leste' not verified. Tetun: 'ida-mesak' for 'unitário' follows Art. 1.
- **Semi-presidential system and separation of powers** (`tl.politics.system`, draft, 6 sources): Check: that the VII Government's programme and budget were not approved in 2017–2018 (wording kept general); 'only semi-presidential republic in Southeast Asia' comes from an FES paper seen in search results — keep or remove; dissolution date 26 January 2018 from Wikipedia/La'o Hamutuk search summaries. Tetun: 'hakotu Parlamentu (disolusaun)' follows Art. 86; 'mosaun sensura' follows Art. 111.
- **The President of the Republic** (`tl.politics.president_office`, draft, 15 sources): Updated 2026-11-11. Verified on presidenciarepublica.tl: Casa Civil head Henriqueta Maria da Silva (Decree of the President No. 32/2026, announced 17 March 2026) and deputy Graziela Fátima Liu Soares (Decree 33/2026); Jesuína Maria Ferreira Gomes was Chief of Staff from 11 July 2023 until she became Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs on 5 March 2026; Leonela do Rosário Martins deputy head of the Casa Militar (Decree 96/2026, 27 August 2026; a Dec 2025 post names Major Francisco da Silva as head of the Casa Militar, not used in the text); Lere Anan Timur called 'Principal Presidential Advisor' (Oct 2026 posts). The organic law number (Lei 3/2011, amended by Lei 1/2014) comes only from a search summary of Jornal da República Série I n.º 20 A (2017) — mj.gov.tl was not reachable; please check. Palace facts (foundation 2 July 2007, inaugurated 27 Aug 2009, Chinese-funded; Palácio das Cinzas unveiled 28 Oct 2002) from English Wikipedia only. 'Presidential election planned for March 2027' is from the Court of Appeal site (EU visit, 26 Oct 2026); the exact date was not found. REMOVED the old claim that Ramos-Horta 'cannot stand again': Art. 75(3) allows one renewal, but whether non-consecutive terms count is debated (a Tatoli opinion piece, Feb 2026, speaks of 'constitutional silence'; en.tatoli.tl was blocked) — a reviewer should decide. Kept from before: acting Presidents in 2008 (Vicente Guterres 11–13 Feb, then Fernando 'La Sama' de Araújo, per leaders.json). Wiki: en/pt titles checked (old ones were redirects); id title not checked (id.wikipedia blocked). Tetun to check: 'Kaza Sivíl', 'Kaza Militár', 'xefe-adjunta', 'Superintendente-Asistente', 'jenerál reformadu', 'ahi han' (burnt).
- **The National Parliament** (`tl.politics.parliament`, draft, 16 sources): Updated 2026-11-11 from parlamento.tl (história, papel e organização, órgãos, processo legislativo, comissões, bancadas, notícias): 88 deputies in 13 groups with 23 women in the first Parliament; session 15 Sept–14 Sept, normal work 15 Sept–15 July; lists of 65 + at least 25 substitutes, d'Hondt, single national constituency; Plenary on Mondays and Tuesdays; quorum rules; Mesa of six (names checked on the Mesa and plenary pages); seven committees A–G; inquiry committees 60 days; legislative process steps; IV session opened 15 Sept 2026; OGE 2027 (US$ 2,568.5 million) approved in general on 11 Nov 2026 by 41 votes, none against, 23 abstentions. DIFFERENCES to check: the site's 'Papel e Organização' page still says one woman in every four candidates, while its history page (and Law 7/2011, per International IDEA) says one in three — text keeps one in three; the history page says 26 women in the current legislature, but the deputies list (Nov 2026) counts 24 women and 41 men (replacements?) — fact keeps '26 elected in 2023'; the site says the Constituent Assembly mandate began on 30 Aug 2001 'with the swearing-in', but the Government archive says it was inaugurated on 15 Sept 2001 (30 Aug 2001 was election day) — text says 'elected in August 2001'. Building history (Indonesian-era building, Australian-funded rehabilitation in 2001) is from the Parliament history page only. Ineligible groups listed from the site (it also lists civil servants, diplomats and CNE members). Ministers' names are from the Government portal composition page (timor-leste.gov.tl/?p=13, read 11 Nov 2026): PM, 2 deputy PMs, 19 ministers, 11 vice-ministers (incl. Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs Jesuína Gomes, sworn in 5 March 2026; Wikipedia's 2023 list has only 10) and 15 secretaries of state. - **Office of the Provedor for Human Rights and Justice (PDHJ)** (`tl.institutions.pdhj`, draft, 17 sources): Facts checked on 2026-11-11 on pdhj.tl (Tetun, Portuguese and English pages) and in the annotated Constitution (Art. 27: elected by an absolute majority of Parliament for four years; the annotation says the Statute, art. 19, allows one renewal). Current leadership comes from the Tetun 'meet the Provedor' page and the home page (2023–2027: Virgílio da Silva Guterres; deputies Maria Marília X. C. de O. da Costa, human rights, and Rigoberto Monteiro, good governance); the English 'meet' page and both Wikipedia articles are out of date (they still show the 2015 or 2019 teams). Discrepancies, written cautiously: (1) the first 'A' accreditation is dated 2007 on the Portuguese history page and in the English sidebar but 2008 in the English history text and on Wikipedia, so the text says 'shortly after opening'; re-accreditation at the end of 2013 is consistent. (2) The date of Law 7/2004 differs (20 May on pdhj.tl; 5 May and 26 May in the annotated Constitution), so only the year is given. (3) The Tetun PDHJ page calls Guterres's mandate 'tinan lima' (five years) for 2023–2027, while Art. 27 says four years; the text says '2023–2027 period'. (4) The history page says the second, successful vote was in March 2005, the 'meet' page says he was appointed in May 2005; both agree he was sworn in in June 2005, so the text gives 2005 and June. (5) Complaint boxes are said to be in the administration of each of the '13 districts' (old map); the text says municipal administrations. Phone numbers were left out because the site gives two different ones. Regional office coverage (organisational structure page): Baucau for Baucau, Lautém, Manatuto, Viqueque; Same for Manufahi, Ainaro, Cova Lima; Maliana for Bobonaro, Ermera, Liquiçá; Oé-Cusse; Dili office for Dili and Aileu. Tetun: pdhj.tl writes the institution 'Provedoria Direitus Umanus no Justisa' (the task brief had 'Direitu Umanu'); the person follows TETUN-STYLE 'Provedór Direitus Umanus no Justisa' (Constitution: 'Provedór ba Direitus Umanus no Justisa'). A native speaker should check 'boa-governasaun', 'keixa' (the site also uses 'kesar'), 'Provedór-Adjuntu', 'dezlokadu', 'merenda eskolár', 're-akreditasaun', 'Asosiasaun Provedór Ázia nian'.

## Presidents and prime ministers (13)

- **Presidents of Timor-Leste (1975, 2002 to today)** (`tl.leaders.presidents`, draft, 11 sources): Sources disagree on 1975–1978: the en.wikipedia list gives Xavier do Amaral 28 Nov–7 Dec 1975 and Lobato from 7 Dec 1975; Lobato's own article and rulers.org say Lobato became President after FRETILIN arrested Amaral in (September) 1977. Text says '1977' and mentions both views. Check that La Sama was abroad on 11 Feb 2008 (reason Vicente Guterres acted for two days). Constitutional facts from Art. 74–77, 84–85 (konstituisaun.txt). Tetun terms: 'Prezidente interinu', 'Administradór Tranzitóriu', 'sidadaun orijinál (nanis)'. Search budget ran out before every date could be re-checked online; dates since 2002 are well documented. Lú-Olo's death on 21 June 2026 confirmed by Tatoli and AP.
- **Prime Ministers of Timor-Leste** (`tl.leaders.prime_ministers`, draft, 9 sources): Lobato's term as Prime Minister: en.wikipedia lists 28 Nov–7 Dec 1975; he kept leading in the resistance and became President in 1977. António Duarte Carvarino 'Mau Lear' (1977–Feb 1979) is taken from the en.wikipedia list of Prime Ministers; check. Ramos-Horta: Alkatiri resigned 26 June 2006, Ramos-Horta sworn in 10 July 2006 (some lists start his term on 26 June). Constitutional rules: Art. 105–108 (konstituisaun.txt). Tetun terms: 'Xefe-Ministru' (Konstituisaun), 'Governu Tranzisaun Daruak', 'Ministru Negósiu Estranjeiru'. Search budget ran out; dates since 2002 come from well-documented lists and should be spot-checked against timor-leste.gov.tl.
- **Constitutional Governments I to IX** (`tl.leaders.governments`, draft, 10 sources): Confirmed online: III (19 May–8 Aug 2007, FRETILIN), IV (8 Aug 2007–8 Aug 2012, AMP), V (8 Aug 2012–16 Feb 2015, CNRT–PD–Frenti-Mudança), VIII (22 Jun 2018–1 Jul 2023; AMP until 25 May 2020; FRETILIN–PLP–KHUNTO–PD from 24 Jun 2020; sources differ on whether PD was a formal member), IX (1 Jul 2023, CNRT–PD, 37 of 65). From documented lists, please spot-check: I (20 May 2002) end date, II start 10 July 2006, VI composition, seat numbers 2007 (FRETILIN 21, CNRT 18, ASDT–PSD 11, PD 8) and 2012 (CNRT 30), VII programme rejected by the opposition (Oct 2017) and dissolution (Jan 2018). Tetun names: 'Aliansa ho Maioria Parlamentár', 'Aliansa Mudansa ba Progresu', 'Organizasaun Mundiál Komérsiu'.
- **Presidents of the National Parliament** (`tl.leaders.parliament_presidents`, draft, 10 sources): Confirmed online: Lú-Olo 2002–2007; La Sama 2007–2012; Vicente Guterres 30 Jul 2012 – 5 May 2016, followed by Adérito Hugo da Costa (not in the brief's list, but in Wikipedia); Aniceto Guterres Lopes elected 19 May 2020 (40 votes, contested; Court of Appeal called it a political act) until 22 Jun 2023; Maria Fernanda Lay from 22 Jun 2023 (45 votes, first woman). Exact start dates of Adérito Hugo da Costa (2016), Aniceto Guterres Lopes (2017) and Arão Noé Amaral (2018) not found — years only. Check: Aniceto Guterres Lopes chaired CAVR; Lú-Olo elected President of the Constituent Assembly in Sept 2001. Constitution: Art. 77, 84, 92–93, 167 (88 deputies in the first legislature).
- **Xanana Gusmão** (`tl.leaders.xanana_gusmao`, draft, 8 sources): Confirmed online: birth 20 June 1946 Laleia (Manatuto); captured 20 Nov 1992; life sentence May 1993, cut to 20 years Aug 1993; house arrest Feb 1999; Sakharov Prize 1999; PM since 1 July 2023 (no 2026 report found that says otherwise). Release date: one source gives 7 Sept 1999 (text says 'September'). From well-documented knowledge, please check: Dare seminary; March 1981 conference choosing him as leader; CNRM year (Konstituisaun preamble says 1987, other sources 1988); CNRT president April 1998; Mandela visit 1997; Sydney Peace Prize 2000; his role as head of the maritime boundary negotiating team. 'Kongresu Nasionál ba Rekonstrusaun Timorense' is a Lafa Tetun rendering of the party name.
- **José Ramos-Horta** (`tl.leaders.ramos_horta`, draft, 7 sources): Confirmed online: birth 26 Dec 1949 Dili; left for New York 4 Dec 1975; Nobel 1996 with Bishop Belo; 2007 run-off ~69%; 2022 run-off 62.1% (Court of Appeal, 29 Apr 2022); UNIOGBIS head and chair of the UN peace operations panel (no dates given, so none in text). Please check: Soibada school, journalist work, Mozambique exile in the early 1970s, Sydney human rights training programme (Diplomacy Training Program), return in Dec 1999, PM sworn in 10 July 2006, 20 May 2022 swearing-in. Tetun: 'Reprezentante Espesiál Sekretáriu-Jerál nian', 'Giné-Bisau', 'Mozambike'.
- **Mari Alkatiri** (`tl.leaders.mari_alkatiri`, draft, 7 sources): Confirmed online: birth 26 Nov 1949 Dili; Hadhrami Arab origin, Muslim; FRETILIN Secretary-General; minister for political affairs in 1975 and left days before the invasion; exile in Mozambique; PM May 2002–26 Jun 2006 and Sept 2017–2018; RAEOA/ZEESM appointment by presidential decree of 25 July 2014 for five years (Lusa). Please check: full name 'Mari bin Amude Alkatiri'; ASDT→FRETILIN in Sept 1974; Petroleum Fund created in 2005 under his government; whether he paused the RAEOA post while PM (2017–2018) — the text avoids saying. Tetun: 'zona espesiál ekonomia sosiál merkadu' for ZEESM.
- **Taur Matan Ruak** (`tl.leaders.taur_matan_ruak`, draft, 7 sources): Confirmed online: birth name and date (10 Oct 1956); captured 1979, escaped after 23 days; last FALINTIL commander; first F-FDTL commander from 1 Feb 2001 (brigadier general) to 2011; 2012 run-off 16 April, 61.23%, independent backed by CNRT; PM 22 Jun 2018 – 1 Jul 2023. Sources differ on when he took over FALINTIL (1998 after Konis Santana's death vs after the 1999 vote); timeline uses 1998. Please check: birthplace Uaigia/Baguia (Baucau); meaning 'two sharp eyes'; cantonment in Aileu in 1999; PLP leadership; April 2021 floods. Tetun: 'matan rua kro'at', 'kampu kantonamentu', 'Brigadeiru-Jenerál'.
- **Francisco Guterres Lú-Olo** (`tl.leaders.lu_olo`, draft, 7 sources): Confirmed online: birth 7 Sept 1954 Ossu (Viqueque); speaker 2002–2007 and President of the Constituent Assembly; President 2017–2022; lost run-offs 2007 and 2012; died 21 June 2026 in Kuala Lumpur (Prince Court Medical Centre), aged 71; seven days of national mourning 22–28 June; buried 26 June 2026 at the Garden of Heroes and Martyrs of the Homeland, Metinaro; FALINTIL flag handed to a son. Please check: FRETILIN president from 2001; joining FRETILIN in 1974–75; 2017 first-round win (about 57%); the 2018 refusal to appoint some proposed ministers (reported for the VIII Government). Tetun: 'Jardín Asuwa'in no Martir Pátria nian' is a Lafa rendering of the official name.
- **Francisco Xavier do Amaral** (`tl.leaders.xavier_do_amaral`, draft, 7 sources): Confirmed online: born 3 Dec 1937 Turiscai (some sources 1939); Mambai; founded ASDT 1974 (with Ramos-Horta and Lobato); President from 28 Nov 1975; arrested by FRETILIN 1977; captured by Indonesian troops Aug 1978; Bali, then Jakarta 1983; long years under an Indonesian general; returned after 1999; Constituent Assembly 2001; deputy speaker 2002 (government statement); candidate 2002 (17.3%, won Aileu), 2007, registered for 2012; died 6 Mar 2012 in Dili aged 74; buried Jardim dos Heróis, Metinaro; monument in Turiscai (Tatoli). An AP report (ETAN, 2005) says he resigned from the legislature in 2005 — text does not claim he sat until 2012. Nickname: sources give 'Avó/Abo Xavier'. Check the reason for the 1977 split (text stays general: protecting civilians).
- **Nicolau Lobato** (`tl.leaders.nicolau_lobato`, draft, 7 sources): Confirmed online: born 24 May 1946 Soibada; first PM 28 Nov 1975; President from 1977 (after Amaral's arrest; some lists say from Dec 1975); killed 31 Dec 1978 at Mindelo (sources place Mindelo between Turiscai/Maubisse and Same); remains never confirmed; National Heroes Day on 31 Dec since 2017 (7 Dec renamed Memorial Day); statue/roundabout in Dili; new Soibada statue completed Aug 2026. Please check: wife Isabel killed on the Dili wharf on 8 Dec 1975; 'vice-president of FRETILIN'; 'Catholic schools' (some sources say the Dare seminary); airport name (from the Wikipedia page title, not re-checked online). Some reports name the Indonesian unit and officer involved — left out on purpose.
- **Rui Maria de Araújo** (`tl.leaders.rui_araujo`, draft, 6 sources): Confirmed online: born 21 May 1964 in Mape, Cova Lima (NOT Turiscai); Minister of Health from 20 Sept 2001 (to 2006/2007 — sources differ); Deputy PM for social affairs 2006–2007; Council of State 2007–2012; joined FRETILIN 2010, Central Committee 2011; PM 16 Feb 2015 – 15 Sept 2017; Minister of Health again Sept 2017 – June 2018. Medical school differs between sources (Sultan Agung Islamic University, Semarang, in the text of en.wikipedia; Udayana in the infobox), so the text only says 'in Indonesia'. 'Qualified in the 1990s' is based on one source giving 1994. No information found on his activities after 2018. 'First Minister of Health' follows the en.wikipedia list of health ministers, which starts with him in Sept 2001 (the 2000–2001 transitional cabinet had no health minister); please confirm.
- **National heroes and figures of the resistance** (`tl.leaders.national_heroes`, draft, 12 sources): Art. 11 quoted from konstituisaun.txt (Tetun title 'Fó valór ba Rezisténsia'). Confirmed online: Law 3/2006 amended by Laws 9/2009, 2/2011 and 3/2024 (12 June 2024); presidential decrees grant state honours and burial at Metinaro (Art. 23(1)(f) of the statute); municipal 'Jardim dos Heróis da Pátria'; 2008 list of 15 leading figures headed by Xavier do Amaral and including Lobato, Mau Lear, Vicente 'Sahe', Konis Santana, David Alex (the full list was not found); Konis Santana led FALINTIL Apr 1993 – Mar 1998; Rosa Bonaparte OPMT secretary-general Aug 1975, killed 8 Dec 1975; Martinho da Costa Lopes died in Portugal 27 Feb 1991, 'voz dos sem voz'; journalists honoured posthumously Oct 2025; 31 Dec Heroes Day since 2017. Not re-checked online: Sebastião Gomes (28 Oct 1991, Motael) — well documented in the Santa Cruz article; Konis Santana from Tutuala; Borja da Costa killed 7 Dec 1975 and author of 'Foho Ramelau'; 3 March Veterans' Day; first national park. The brief said 'Bishop' Martinho, but he was Apostolic Administrator (Monsignor), not a bishop — text says Monsignor.

## National symbols (8)

- **The national flag** (`tl.symbols.flag`, draft, 5 sources): Colour meanings quoted word for word from Konstituisaun Artigu 15 (Tetun edition, local copy); star = 'naroman ne'ebé hatudu dalan'. English/Portuguese/Indonesian glosses are our paraphrase, not the official PT text. Art. 156(1)(i) and 156(2): flag may only be revised by national referendum — check wording. Law 2/2007 description of the flag (sovereignty, independence, unity, territorial integrity) from WIPO Lex summary. Britannica gives a different, unofficial reading of the colours; we only mention that other readings exist. Restoration ceremony time ('midnight 19/20 May 2002', Tasitolu) — check. Tetun terms: ai-riin (flagpole), dezeñu, kalan-boot (midnight), Nasoins Unidas.
- **The national emblem** (`tl.symbols.emblem`, draft, 6 sources): 2026-11-11: sections 'What the emblem shows' and 'What the parts mean' rewritten after the Government's official description (timor-leste.gov.tl ?p=34, in pt/tet/en), which confirms the four angles = separation of powers and interdependence of the organs of sovereignty, the rifle model 'AK-47/Galaxi', and diman = 'flecha' (arrow) — the English page says 'spear', the Portuguese and Tetun pages say arrow, as does the image. The owner supplied the official emblem image (motto 'Unidade, Acção, Progresso'), now shown on the National Symbols page. Date of Law 2/2007: WIPO Lex lists adoption 17 Jan 2007 and entry into force 19 Jan 2007; Wikipedia and a 2025 decree cite 'Lei n.º 2/2007, de 18 de janeiro' — we use 18 January. Description and meanings follow Wikipedia and the FOTW translation of the law (not the original PT text). 'Based on a 1975 emblem' only from Heraldry-Wiki/WorldAtlas summaries — check or drop. 'Belak' as the emblem's name: Wikipedia/FOTW. Rifle described as Kalashnikov in sources; we write 'rifle'. Tetun terms to check: roda-dente (cogwheel), hare-fuan, batar-fuan, karimbu (stamp), rama-inan (bow), diman (spear), hirus-matan (chest), brazaun, eskudu.
- **The national anthem: Pátria** (`tl.symbols.anthem`, draft, 4 sources): Composer: the brief writes 'Afonso Redentor de Araújo'; Wikipedia and national-anthems.org write 'Afonso Redentor Araújo' — choose one spelling. Borja da Costa 'killed during the invasion' (Wikipedia); exact day (often given as 7 Dec 1975) not confirmed in this session, so we only say December 1975. 'A Tetun version exists' per Wikipedia. Art. 14 and Art. 166 checked in the local Tetun Konstituisaun; Art. 166 melody title quoted only in pt/id/en ('Pátria, Pátria, Timor-Leste a Nossa Nação'). Law 2/2007 lists the anthem among the 3 symbols (WIPO summary) — check whether it fixes the official text/score. Only one short line of the lyrics is quoted. Etiquette in section 4 is general guidance, not quoted from the law.
- **The motto: Unidade, Acção, Progresso** (`tl.symbols.motto`, draft, 4 sources): 'Core values of the nation's political and ethical life' follows the FOTW translation of the emblem law, not the original Portuguese — check against Lei n.º 2/2007. Motto on a white wavy ribbon in red letters (Wikipedia/FOTW). The explanations of unity/action/progress for learners are our own teaching text. CNRT founded in 1998 (month not given). Art. 19 checked in the local Tetun Konstituisaun. Tetun: kurva hanesan laloran (wavy), hanoin-hikas (remind).
- **Currency: the US dollar and centavo coins** (`tl.symbols.currency`, draft, 6 sources): 100-centavo coin: Wikipedia gives 2013 as introduction, Numista lists coins dated 2012 — we write 2013. 25-centavo design: BCTL photo page calls it a port/boat scene, dealers a boat — we write 'a boat'. Coin designs from BCTL/Numista summaries. Colonial currencies (pataca, escudo) deliberately not named. BPA 2001–2011 and BCTL from 13 Sep 2011 (Law 5/2011) from bancocentral.tl, Wikipedia and a Mondaq summary. Minting in Lisbon (INCM) from Wikipedia. Tetun terms: troku (change), osan-surat (banknote), osan-besi (coin), emite, kakuluk nautilu, manu-aman futu nian (fighting rooster), kaibauk 'ho forma kurva hanesan fulan'.
- **Official and working languages** (`tl.symbols.languages_official`, draft, 5 sources): Articles 8(3), 13 and 159 checked in the local Tetun Konstituisaun; the Tetun quotes are word for word, the other languages paraphrase. Number of national languages: sources differ (Wikipedia lists about 15 recognised; others count about 30 languages and dialects) — we say 'around thirty by some counts'. INL spelling adopted by the Government in 2004 (Decree 1/2004) — confirm. EMBLI start year (2012) and 'some schools' taken from general knowledge of the pilot; the cited summary was seen only as a search result. CPLP membership 2002 and ASEAN membership October 2025 not re-checked in this session. Tetun: 'lian serbisu' follows Art. 159 (alternative spelling: servisu).
- **Dili, the capital** (`tl.symbols.capital_dili`, draft, 5 sources): Dili municipality population '324 thousand' (2022) from the UNFPA/INETL preliminary-results summary — check the exact final figure; 'about one in four' is 324 000 / 1 340 434. 10 October 1769 and Governor Teles de Meneses from the old Lafa data and Wikipedia (not re-verified in this session); Topasses as attackers of Lifau — check wording. WWII damage, Tasitolu Masses (1989, 2024), April 2021 floods and Ataúro municipality (2022) not re-verified here. Tibar Bay port 'a short way west' of Dili (it may lie in Liquiçá municipality). Tetun: rai-ponta (cape), komarka (prison), bee-sa'e (flood), foer (rubbish).
- **The name Timor-Leste** (`tl.symbols.name`, draft, 5 sources): Etymology (Malay timur = east) as given by Wikipedia; the explanation 'seen from Java' is our simplification. Law 2/2007: name of the Republic belongs exclusively to the State (WIPO Lex summary). 'Timor Timur' (province name 1976–1999), ISO code TL, .tl domain and UN membership on 27 Sep 2002 not re-checked in this session. Crocodile legend: many versions exist; keep consistent with tl.culture.legends. 'Abó' (grandfather) — some write avó/avô. In Tetun text we avoid the Portuguese spelling 'Timor-Leste' (checker) and write Timór-Leste / Timór Lorosa'e.

## Map, municipalities and islands (19)

- **The map of Timor-Leste** (`tl.geography.map`, draft, 6 sources): Area: sources give 14 870–15 010 km² (used ≈14 950 / about 15 000). Ramelau 2 986 m (some sources 2 963 m). Coastline ≈700 km and main-part length ≈265 km come from the old Lafa atlas; check. 1859/1904/1914 border steps and the 27th-province claim (1976) are well known, but I could only check them against the Wikipedia pages listed. The web-search budget ran out during this task, so I could not open these pages again. Tetun: 'tasi-le'un', 'rai-le'un' and 'kalohan-laran' follow Constitution art. 4. 'Sunda Ki'ik' (Lesser Sunda) is my own term; check it.
- **Administrative divisions** (`tl.geography.administrative_divisions`, draft, 6 sources): Counts: the 2020 OCHA data has 65 posts (incl. 'Atauro' as one post of Dili); after 2022 Ataúro's sucos may have become posts, so I wrote 'around 65 to 70'. Sucos ≈450 (442 in older data, 452 after later changes?) and aldeias >2 200: check the current Ministry of State Administration figures. RAEOA created by Law n.º 3/2014 (June 2014) – number/date from memory, so the text only says 'a law in 2014'. 'Districts renamed municipalities in the 2010s' kept vague on purpose (2014–2016 decree-laws). Ataúro municipality from 1 January 2022 matches the old Lafa atlas and the map notes, but I could not find a source for it with web search (the search budget ran out). Tetun: 'zona espesiál ekonomia sosiál merkadu nian' is my rendering of ZEESM.
- **Islands: Ataúro, Jaco, Fatu Sinai and the Oé-Cusse enclave** (`tl.geography.islands`, draft, 6 sources): Fatu Sinai / Pulau Batek: I wrote 'claimed by both, in practice under Indonesian control, linked to sea-border talks'. This is from memory and not verified (the web-search budget ran out), so check the current status. Ataúro: ≈140 km²; Manucoco 'almost 1 000 m' (sources 995–999 m); ≈10 000 people (2022 census, my figure 10 302 not verified); village names in pt spelling (tet Makadade/Makili?). 'Richest reefs ever surveyed' refers to the 2016 Conservation International reef-fish survey. Jaco ≈11 km². NKS park 2007 (some sources 2008). Lifau 'around 1515'. Governor Lemos Pires moved to Ataúro on 26–27 Aug 1975 and left on 7–8 Dec 1975 (from memory).
- **The land border with Indonesia** (`tl.geography.border`, draft, 6 sources): Border length 'roughly 250 km' (figures of about 250–270 km circulate) — check. The Maucatar/Noimuti swap in the 1904 convention and the 1914 arbitration are from memory. So are the 2005 provisional agreement, the open segments (Noel Besi–Citrana, Bidjael Sunan–Oben) and the July 2019 agreement in principle; check whether a final text has been signed since. Crossing names Sakato–Wini and Oesilo–Napan are from memory. ≈250 000 people taken or fled to West Timor in 1999 is the usual UNHCR estimate. The web-search budget ran out, so these points rest on the Wikipedia pages listed and on memory. Tetun: 'foho-leten sira' used for 'mountain ridges'.
- **Regions: north coast, south coast, mountains and the east** (`tl.geography.regions`, draft, 5 sources): General climate pattern (north drier, rain mainly Dec–Apr; south coast with a second rainy period in mid-year) is standard, but align it with writer A's climate article. 'More than 40 000 years' near Tutuala (Jerimalai) is used to match other articles, which say about 42 000–44 000. Ira Lalaro is the largest lake. Tetun terms to check: 'ai-akadiru' (lontar palm), 'ai-parapa' (mangrove, as in old Lafa text), 'abuabu' (fog), 'ruin-kotuk' (spine), 'tun-sa'e makaas' (steep). The web-search budget ran out, so I did not add new sources.
- **Aileu** (`mun.aileu`, draft, 13 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (two new sections, facts, timeline, highlights, two questions). NUMBERS: population 54 324 = 2022 census final result (INETL, as in the en Wikipedia table 'Municipalities of Timor-Leste' that cites INETL; the 14 final figures add up to the national 1 341 737). The old value 54 293 had no source; the preliminary result (DGE, Nov 2022) was 54 631. Area 676 km² from the census table (same page; also tet Wikipedia 676.02); the en 'Aileu Municipality' text still gives 737 km² (2015) and the Lafa map polygons ≈ 736 km², so the area changed with new boundaries — check. Density 54 324 / 676 ≈ 80. Sucos/aldeias 33/139 (2022) and 35/143 (2024) are from the INETL yearbooks Aileu em Números as seen in web-search summaries (the INETL site is blocked in the sandbox; per-post sums checked); the en Wikipedia list also has 33; older pt/tet Wikipedia say 31 sucos, 135 aldeias. Maubohilau 2 312 m from timorleste.tl 'Five Summits'. 47 km from timorleste.tl and en Wikipedia 'Aileu'. 1903 revolt, January 1912 base, 31 Aug 1942 attack, end-1999 capital plans, Seloi Craic 6 Sep 1999 from en Wikipedia 'Aileu'; 'Vila General Carmona' now confirmed there. Market days, 28 places to stay, 9 restaurants from the timorleste.tl Aileu page (Nov 2026). Hume/Moreland friendship (May 2000) from en 'Aileu Municipality'. 'Aileu modo metan' from tet Wikipedia (unreferenced) — check. STILL UNVERIFIED in the older text: FRETILIN/FALINTIL base in 1975 and the FALINTIL cantonment at Aileu (1999–2001) — not found in the en 'Falintil' page; check. Tetun to check: 'rai-kle'an', 'ai-laran kafé nian', 'foin sai konsellu rasik'.
- **Ainaro** (`mun.ainaro`, draft, 14 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (economy and administration sections, facts, timeline, highlights, two questions). NUMBERS: population 73 115 = 2022 census final result (INETL, via the en Wikipedia table that cites INETL; the 14 final figures add up to 1 341 737); the old value 72 989 was the preliminary result (DGE, Nov 2022). Area 869.8 km² from the 2022 census table (same page); the old value 869 was this figure rounded, but en Wikipedia text gives 804 km², pt/gov 797 km² and the Lafa map polygons ≈ 802 km² (borders changed) — check. Density ≈ 84. 21 sucos and 131 aldeias read in pt Wikipedia; the per-post split (7/9/3/2; aldeias 31/63/21/16) is from Ainaro em Números 2021 as seen in a web-search summary (INETL site blocked in the sandbox). Mother tongues 2015 and the 2003/Indonesian-era border changes, 'ai naruk' and Orluli from en 'Ainaro Municipality'. HEIGHT: en Wikipedia 'Tatamailau' and timorleste.tl give 2 963 m, the government page 2 960 m; the older section text and geography.json say 2 986 m — the facts give both; harmonise. Statue installed 1997 (en 'Tatamailau'). Coffee/ACTL, pack horses, ASTRABEKA, 33 places to stay, 14 restaurants, Maubisse 70 km / 2–3 h from timorleste.tl (Nov 2026). STILL UNVERIFIED in the older text: Dom Aleixo Corte-Real (liurai of Suro) killed by Japanese forces in 1943; cabbage and potatoes. The old map note (Zumalai under Ainaro) no longer applies: posts.json now lists Zumalai under Cova Lima. Tetun to check: 'kuda-oan', 'bainaka', 'rai bokur', 'repolu', 'fehuk-ropa', 'pouzada'.
- **Ataúro** (`mun.atauro`, draft, 14 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (economy and administration sections, facts, timeline, highlights, one question). NUMBERS: population 10 295 = 2022 census final result (INETL, via the en Wikipedia table that cites INETL); the old value 10 302 was the preliminary result. Area 140.5 km² from the 2022 census table (also tet Wikipedia 140.50); en Wikipedia text says 150 km² and pt Wikipedia about 117 km² — sources differ. Density ≈ 73. Manucoco 999 m (en) / 995 m (pt). 23.5 km and the 2-hour Berlin Nakroma ferry from en 'Atauro' (pt says about 25 km). POSTS: the 'posts' list (kept unchanged) names the five SUCOS of Ataúro (en/pt Wikipedia); whether Law 14/2021 made them administrative posts could not be checked (the law text and atauro.gov.tl are not reachable), so no number of posts is given in the facts — check and fix the list if Ataúro has one post or none. Law 14/2021 of 7 July (amending Law 11/2009, approved 31 May 2021, in force 1 Jan 2022) from pt Wikipedia; Decree-Laws 4/2022 (municipal administration) and 24/2022 (Special Development Fund) seen only as titles/summaries in web-search results. Water, ferry, 1884 flag, three domains, Protestant north (Dutch Calvinist mission from Alor) from en Wikipedia 'Atauro'; crafts, 27 places to stay, 5 restaurants from timorleste.tl (Nov 2026). STILL UNVERIFIED in the older text: 'thousands' of Timorese, many of them guerrillas' relatives, held on Ataúro in the early 1980s (en Wikipedia only says the island was used as a prison for guerrillas). Tetun to check: 'ró-ahi', 'bijuteria', 'domíniu', 'boneka hena', 'mergullu', 'eko-lodge'.

- **Bobonaro** (`mun.bobonaro`, draft, 12 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (economy and administration sections, facts, timeline, highlights, two questions). - **Cova Lima** (`mun.covalima`, draft, 13 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (economy and administration sections, facts, timeline, highlights, two questions). NUMBERS: population 73 933 = 2022 census final result (INETL, via the en Wikipedia table that cites INETL); the old value 73 909 was the preliminary result. Area 1 206.7 km² from the 2022 census table (tet Wikipedia 1 206.66; old value 1 226 = pt Wikipedia/government page; en text 1 230) — sources differ. Density ≈ 61. 30 sucos (en Wikipedia list and tet Wikipedia). 136 km (en 'Cova Lima Municipality', government page) or 138 km (en 'Suai', pt Wikipedia). Decree 43 889 of 1 Sep 1961 from the en Wikipedia municipality table (it cites Diário da República). Toponymy from en Wikipedia; languages, rice plains/coffee hills and climate from tet Wikipedia (unreferenced — check); airport enlarged 2017 and referral hospital from en 'Suai'; up to 200 killed in the church (UN inquiry) from en 'Suai Church massacre'; beaches, marshes, 19 places to stay, 9 restaurants from timorleste.tl. Highest point not found in the sources used. STILL UNVERIFIED in the older text: the 1904 Maucatar/Noimuti exchange, the Salele–Motamasin crossing, the current status of the Tasi Mane supply base. Post spellings 'Fohorem', 'Maucatar' kept (the map file has 'Forohem', 'Maukatar'); the map now lists Zumalai under Cova Lima, so that old map note no longer applies. Tetun to check: 'rai-tetuk', 'rai-tahu tasi nian' (salt marsh), 'ospitál referál', 'amu-lulik', 'baze apoiu'.
- **Dili** (`mun.dili`, draft, 15 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (economy and administration sections, facts, timeline, highlights, one question). NUMBERS: population 324 738 = 2022 census final result (INETL, via the en Wikipedia table that cites INETL; INETL 'Dili em Números 2022' gives the same per a search summary); the old value 324 269 was the preliminary result. Area 227.6 km² from the 2022 census table — it replaces the old estimate of 228 km² (en 'Dili Municipality' says 224.0 km²). Density ≈ 1 427. 31 sucos and 'second smallest by area' from en 'Dili Municipality'. Economic hub since 1769, half of non-oil GDP and 8th-highest living costs (2006), visitors 14 000 → 51 000 (2006–2013), 28 Nov and 7 Dec 1975 from en 'Dili'; Boeing 737 limit from en 'Baucau Municipality'; 90 places to stay and 63 restaurants from timorleste.tl (Nov 2026); Santa Cruz (12 Nov 1991, at least 250 killed) from en 'Santa Cruz massacre'. FIRST COUNCIL: 1940 on the government page, 1941 in the en Wikipedia table, so the text says 'early 1940s'. STILL UNVERIFIED in the older text: the heroes' cemetery at Metinaro. The old map note (Dom Aleixo under Liquiçá) no longer applies: posts.json now lists Dom Aleixo under Dili. Tetun to check: 'PIB ne'ebé la mai husi mina', 'paraketista', 'kuartu ida'.
- **Ermera** (`mun.ermera`, draft, 10 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (economy and administration sections, facts, timeline, highlights, two questions). DISTANCE not given: en 'Gleno' says 30 km from Dili (straight line?), the government page 58 km. STILL UNVERIFIED in the older text: SAPT plantations, harvest from mid-year, Atsabe as seat of a Kemak kingdom, colonial buildings in Ermera town, Timor Hybrid details. - **Lautém** (`mun.lautem`, draft, 13 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (economy and administration sections, facts, timeline, highlights, two questions). - **Liquiçá** (`mun.liquica`, draft, 14 sources): Profile completed 2026-11-11 (economy and administration sections, facts, timeline, highlights, two questions). NOTE: the older history text says the Dutch built the Maubara fort in the 18th century and that timorleste.tl calls it a '17th-century Dutch fort' — check. ## Culture and tradition (17)

- **Culture of Timor-Leste** (`tl.culture.overview`, draft, 7 sources): Overview linking the other culture articles. Check: 'more than a dozen language groups' (see tl.culture.ethnic_groups / tl.society.languages for exact figures); 'great majority Catholic' kept without a number; papal visits (Oct 1989, Sept 2024; Wikipedia 'Tasitolu' gives about 600,000 at the 2024 Mass) should match tl.culture.catholic_traditions; 'Loron Matebian' used as the Tetun name of All Souls' Day; the Church's role is stated in the Constitution's preamble.
- **Tais: woven cloth** (`tl.culture.tais`, draft, 6 sources): Based on the 2021 Lafa article, corrected and expanded. Check: Tetun plant names 'indigu' and 'ai-morinda' (local names may differ, e.g. tarun/nonu); regional styles (Lautém black-red stripes, Oé-Cusse floral/religious motifs, Bobonaro mud dye) summarised from The Textile Atlas and museum notes; US$265,895 is the amount requested in decision 16.COM 8.a.6 (news calls it a grant); 2020 nomination year from timorleste.tl; 'selendang' popularity from Wikipedia. Gift directions in marriage exchanges vary by community. Web-search budget ran out during this session, so no further cross-checks were possible.
- **Uma lulik: sacred houses** (`tl.culture.uma_lulik`, draft, 6 sources): Based on the 2021 Lafa article, corrected and expanded. Fuiloro roof height (7–9 m) and renewal cycle (10–20 years) come from one 2021 study (MODUL journal) and apply to that community only. Heirloom list varies by family; 'morten' and 'belak' as heirlooms should be checked by a local reviewer. Presidential Palace roof design from Wikipedia. Web-search budget ran out during this session.
- **Lisan, customary law and tara bandu** (`tl.culture.lisan`, draft, 9 sources): Check: the claim that tara bandu was forbidden during the occupation (Mongabay/Wikipedia); 2023 MAF list of marine tara bandu sites (Coral Triangle Center); the lia-na'in seat and election by the suco council (Law 9/2016, art. 10 and 12 as cited by a Manatuto guide); Decree-Law 26/2012 recognition of tara bandu (ADB summary). Tetun terms: 'lia-lisan' (ritual speech), 'habelar biti', 'rai-na'in/tasi-na'in'. Customs and penalties vary by community.
- **Marriage and barlake** (`tl.culture.marriage_barlake`, draft, 6 sources): Sensitive topic: please have a Timorese reviewer check tone. Spelling of 'fetosaa' varies (fetosan, fetosaan). 'About half of marriages' is an estimate quoted by IIAS (2019). Gift directions (buffalo/horses/metal from the groom's side; tais/pigs from the bride's side) are general patterns and vary by community. Uncles leading negotiations and the smaller gift to the bride's mother in matrilineal communities come from Wikipedia. The 1970s Dili 'barlake' debate comes from IIAS. Tetun 'karau' used for buffalo.
- **Dances: tebe, likurai, dahur and bidu** (`tl.culture.dances`, draft, 6 sources): Based on the 2021 Lafa article 'tebe, bidu, likurai', corrected and expanded. Only one source (Tekee Media) describes dahur (sung dance, usually without instruments) — a dance specialist should add where it is danced. The bidu description (slow steps, hand movements, scarves, guitars) comes from the old Lafa text and needs a source. 'Most widespread dance' for likurai and the warriors' welcome come from Wikipedia; the July 2026 Fulan Fehan performance from RRI. Tetun 'tala' (gong) from Tekee Media.
- **Music and instruments** (`tl.culture.music`, draft, 6 sources): Based on the 2021 Lafa article (babadok, lakadou) and Wikipedia. Check: babadok size 30–50 cm and the dadir (flat gong, about 25 cm) from Wikipedia 'Music of Timor-Leste'; 'tala' = gong (Tekee Media). 'Kakalo' was requested but no source could confirm what it is, so it is left out. 'Pátria' sung on 28 Nov 1975: standard account (Wikipedia), to be cross-checked with tl.symbols.anthem; Wikipedia gives 8 Dec 1975 for Borja da Costa's death, so the text says 'December 1975'. Guitar/violin as Portuguese introductions is a general statement.
- **Legends and oral tradition** (`tl.culture.legends`, draft, 7 sources): Story text based on the 2021 Lafa article 'lafaek legend', checked against Wikipedia 'Lafaek Diak' (wise monkey episode) and timorleste.tl (legs = peninsulas, tail = eastern tip). 'Tasi feto' (calm northern sea, Ombai–Wetar) and 'tasi mane' (rough southern Timor Sea) confirmed by Wikipedia 'Ombai Strait' and other sites; Jaco sacred to the Fataluku comes from the old Lafa atlas; Matebian peak names Mane/Feto and meaning from Wikipedia and mountaineering sites. Tetun 'laran-sadi' (pity) and 'lekirauk' (monkey) to be checked.
- **Food and cuisine** (`tl.culture.food`, draft, 7 sources): Check: ikan sabuko fish (sources say 'mackerel', often Spanish mackerel); tukir meat (goat, beef or lamb – sources differ) and spices from Jawa Pos; budu has two descriptions (fresh tomato-mint sauce per Wikipedia, fermented fish sauce per a travel site); hungry season Nov–Feb from Wikipedia; tua sabu 38–54% from a 2025 study of samples at production sites; 'bua no malus' as a welcome is common practice but the Tetun term was not found in a source; Timor Hybrid and rust resistance is general knowledge (see tl.economy.coffee). Tetun food words to check: fore mungu, fehuk-midar, ortelán, liis-mean, derok-tahan, ai-suar, reseita.
- **Crafts: pottery, baskets, carving, silver** (`tl.culture.crafts`, draft, 9 sources): New article. Check: pottery centres (Manatuto, Oé-Cusse) from timorleste.tl; '30 villages in the 1970s' from an IRD-listed 2014 publication (title not seen); ETDA glazed-pottery training from timorleste.tl 'Pride and Pottery'; Ataúro carving from Artlink/QAGOMA/CDU; silversmithing not known in all communities from a Singapore museum record. 'Most artisans were women' refers to an EU project (65% in Dili, Ataúro, Liquiçá, Manatuto). Tetun craft words to check: luhu (basket), rai-barru (clay), kontas (beads), kadeli (ring), brinku (earring), tabuleiru.
- **Traditional dress and adornment (belak, kaibauk)** (`tl.culture.dress_adornment`, draft, 5 sources): Kaibauk and belak meanings (strength/protection; moon, peace, prosperity, fertility; male/female balance) and 'kaibauk on the centavo coins' come from Wikipedia 'Kaibauk' as seen in search results — check which coins show it. One source says women wear the kaibauk to hold their hair; kept as 'sources differ'. Materials (gold, silver, bronze) and the jeweller Joaquim Surumali from RTP (article date not seen). Morten (often orange, luck, protection from bad spirits) from a UKDW thesis and RTP; spelling varies (morten/morteng/mortene). Men's sword (surik) and metal bracelets ('lokum/kelui' in one thesis, not used) from the UKDW thesis. Feathers only sourced as 'worn with tais in ceremonies' (Wikipedia 'Tais'); no source described a specific feather headdress, so the text stays general. Dance details (likurai drum) follow the dance article. Tetun terms to check: hirus-matan (chest), fulan foun (crescent), osan-besi sentavu, kontas, korál, badaen, hado'ok.
- **Peoples and ethnolinguistic groups** (`tl.culture.ethnic_groups`, draft, 8 sources): Written to complement tl.society.languages (which has the census table and language map); group locations follow Wikipedia and SEAsite. Check with a linguist: Waima'a (Baucau/Vemasse), Naueti (south coast of Viqueque), Galolen (north coast near Manatuto), Idalaka ('central mountains' only), Habun (location left out on purpose), Ataúro varieties related to Wetar, and 'Kawaimina' as Ka(irui)-Wai(ma'a)-Mi(diki)-Na(ueti) (from general linguistic sources, not seen in this session's searches). 2015 mother-tongue order (Tetun Prasa, Mambai, Makasae, Tetun Terik, Baikeno) as in tl.society.languages (MRG/SBS); 2022 census language tables were searched for but not found, so no 2022 figures are given. Group population figures differ widely between sources (e.g. WorldAtlas vs SEAsite), so none are given. Chinese-Timorese (mostly Hakka) and mixed Timorese-Portuguese families from Wikipedia 'Timor-Leste' (not re-read). Wiki title 'Demographics of Timor-Leste' assumed after Wikipedia's renaming. Tetun terms: linguista, lian-inan, Timór Osidentál, tasi-ibun sul/norte, la'ós-Austronézia.
- **Catholic faith and traditions** (`tl.culture.catholic_traditions`, draft, 11 sources): Dominican start on Solor: 1556 in one Wikipedia page, 1562 in another — both given. 1641 baptism of the Ambeno royal family at Lifau (Wikipedia 'Lifau', 'History of Timor-Leste'). Senhor Morto procession in Oecusse described as centuries old (UCA News 2020). Catholic share 20–30% (1975) and ~90% (1994) from Wikipedia; today 97–98% (2015 census about 97.6%; a Church directory gives 98.3% for 2016) — check the 2022 census religion table. Tetun liturgy: Tatoli (2026) says 'between 1981 and 1983'; text says 'early 1980s'. The Indonesian rule that everyone must belong to a recognised religion is from general sources on the occupation (not re-read). Bishop Belo: the 2022 Vatican confirmation of restrictions imposed in 2020 (abuse accusations) is included as one neutral sentence; reviewers may decide whether this suits younger readers. First Timorese cardinal 2022 (Tatoli calls him 'Cardinal'; consistory date not re-checked). Crowd 2024: ~600,000 is the local organisers' estimate quoted by the Vatican spokesman (AP); 'Park of Peace' name from AP. Protestant/Muslim minorities kept without numbers. All Souls' customs (cleaning graves, candles) are general knowledge — check. University Cross since 2013 (Vatican News 2018); youth cross relay between districts (UCAN/ETAN 2007, not cited). Tetun terms to check: Dalan Krus, Domingu Ramus, Loron Matebian, fiel, arkidiosese metropolitana, Parke Dame.
- **Traditional architecture by region** (`tl.culture.architecture`, draft, 7 sources): Fataluku house details (posts over 3 m, steep roof thatched with black palm fibre 'gamuti', 'most unusual structure', seven house types) come from a Google Arts & Culture entry citing Ruy Cinatti, Leopoldo de Almeida and Sousa Mendes (1987) — check the book title (Arquitectura Timorense?) and pages. Pair-house system (uma lulik + rectangular hipped-roof house for living and sleeping) and the conical uma lulik of Mambai, Makasae, Bunak, Kemak (also Atoni, Waima'a, Bekais) from the 2015 ISPRS paper (full title not seen; the paper calls the living house 'uma tidor', not used here). Baucau study (Univ. Évora thesis) on industrial materials replacing traditional ones. Rebuilding of Fataluku origin houses with structural innovations: McWilliam. 'Sustainable architecture' study by Manuel Correia Guedes was only seen as a citation. No source found for Ataúro houses or for the grass name alang-alang/hae, so neither is named. Kept short on ceremonies to avoid repeating tl.culture.uma_lulik. Tetun terms: ahi-matan (hearth), kakuluk ho sorin haat (hipped roof), fibra palmeira, zinku, simente, torre, uma-hun.
- **Sports and traditional games** (`tl.culture.sports_games`, draft, 13 sources): Football: FIFA membership 12 Sep 2005 and AFF debut in 2004 (results table: v Myanmar, 16 Dec 2004) from Wikipedia; 'favourite sport' is general. Olympics: Sydney 2000 as Individual Olympic Athletes with Martinho de Araújo named first (number of athletes not given — check, possibly four); Athens 2004 with two athletics competitors; Paris 2024: four athletes (2 women, 2 men) in athletics, swimming and taekwondo (Tatoli, Wikipedia); Winter Games skier inferred from the existence of 'Timor-Leste at the 2022 Winter Olympics' — check name/years. Tour de Timor: riders 280–350 depending on the report; 12 countries; ~US$75,000 prizes; later editions confirmed for 2010 (bicycles.net.au) but the last year was not found, so none is given. Martial arts: suspensions in 2013 (Council of Ministers) and from November 2023 (Macau Business: at least 4 dead, 26 injured; extended in Dec 2025 to June 2026) — check the current status before publishing; text is kept neutral and names only PSHT and KORK as in the sources. Futu manu: The Conversation (late-afternoon fields, betting, men only, loser eaten, bird-flu risk), Reuters 2012 (government treats it as a traditional game to preserve and regulate), Sciences du Jeu 2015 (sociability/identity). No source found for children's traditional games, so none are described — a local contributor could add some with sources. Tetun terms: pasatempu, gripe manu, eskiadór, Verán/Invernu, prolonga, halterofilista, kamizola.
- **Literature, poetry, film and art** (`tl.culture.arts_literature`, draft, 10 sources): Sylvan: born Dili 26 Aug 1917, died Cascais 25 Dec 1993, president of the Sociedade de Língua Portuguesa (Wikipedia). Borja da Costa: killed in December 1975, as in tl.symbols.anthem (exact day not confirmed). Xanana's poetry: only that scholars compare the sea in his poems and Sylvan's (CITCEM abstract; title of the source given here is descriptive); the book 'Mar Meu' and his paintings were not confirmed, so not named. João Aparício named from the Boletín Galego de Literatura (2018). Cardoso: born 1958 in Cailaco; went to Portugal in 1974; novel titles and dates from search snippets (Wikipedia and studies); Oceanos 2021 and 'first Timorese winner' from PublishNews (Dec 2021). Beatriz's War: premiere 17 Sep 2013 in Dili, ~30,000 viewers at outdoor screenings, Golden Peacock (best film) at the 44th IFFI 2013 — from Wikipedia as summarised; the plot line is a short paraphrase of the Martin Guerre story the film adapts — check. Arte Moris: 2003, Luca Gansser (Gabriela Gansser named in place.arte_moris), former National Museum building in Comoro, eviction Dec 2021 (Wikipedia); 'many best-known artists trained there' follows place.arte_moris. Tetun to check: romansista, hakerek-na'in, realizadór, diretora teatru, tela-boot, Kailaku (Cailaco), ezíliu.
- **The Tetun language** (`tl.culture.tetun`, draft, 10 sources): Overlaps on purpose only lightly with tl.symbols.languages_official (Art. 13/159, INL) and tl.society.languages (census table). Decree date 14 April 2004 (Decreto do Governo n.º 1/2004, on the Tetun orthography) from search snippets of a 2024 study — check the number. 'About 79% can speak Tetun (2015 census)' is the figure 78.78% cited in arXiv 2406.07331 — check the INETL table (speak vs. speak/read/write). Portuguese loans: 32% (newspaper samples) and 35% (interview corpus) in studies cited by the same paper; 'about one third' used. Church liturgy in Tetun 1981–1983 from Tatoli (2026); Wikipedia ('Tetum language', 'Martinho da Costa Lopes') confirms the switch without one exact year. Belu and Malaka regencies from Wikipedia 'Tetun people' (not re-read). 'praça' etymology and Tetun Dili's simplified grammar from Wikipedia. Mutual intelligibility of Terik and Prasa is disputed — kept as 'linguists still discuss'. Tetun Wikipedia cited by its root URL (tet.wikipedia.org); Wikisource Tetun main page seen in search results. Grammar examples (ona, sei, sira, nia, la) are standard Tetun — a native speaker should check the Tetun explanations. Tetun terms to check: glotál, apóstrofu, ortografia padraun, ferramenta, sistema buka, kumprimentu, aban-bainrua.

## Sports (14)

- **Sport in Timor-Leste: an overview** (`tl.sports.overview`, draft, 6 sources): Overview built from the other sports articles (see their notes). Population ~1.3 million is the 2022 census (1 340 434). Dili waterfront jogging and village games are general knowledge, not sourced. NOC recognition 3 Jul 2003 (Olympedia: 115th IOC Session, Prague); FIFA 12 Sep 2005 (Wikipedia, as in tl.culture.sports_games); Asian Games since 2002 (Busan page exists); SEA Games since 2003; first SEA gold 2011 in kempo; 3 SEA golds in total (Wikipedia tally, which reports 3 gold, 10 silver, 43 bronze – check whether it already includes 2025). Web search was limited to 12 grouped queries; fftl.tl was not reachable or returned, so it is not cited. Tetun terms to check: basketból, voleiból, Komité Olímpiku Nasionál, Jogus Desportivus CPLP nian, pasu. EN/ID added 2026-11-11: translated from the Portuguese and checked against the Tetun; same facts, numbers and names; names of clubs, competitions and athletes kept as written; British spelling in EN, EYD in ID, terms as in tl.culture.sports_games (FFTL = Football Federation of Timor-Leste / Federasi Sepak Bola Timor-Leste; IOC for COI; CPLP Games / Pesta Olahraga CPLP). Re-checked 2026-11-11: the 2025 SEA Games brought 1 silver and 7 bronze, no gold, so '3 golds' still stands (Tatoli, 22 Dec 2025). No Timorese entry at Milano Cortina 2026 was found (Olympedia via search lists only 2014, 2018, 2022; no Tatoli report), so 'three times (2014, 2018 and 2022)' was kept — confirm against the official 2026 results.
- **Football: the national teams and the FFTL** (`tl.sports.football`, draft, 9 sources): Checked in search results: AFC decision of 19 Jan 2017 (12 Brazilian-born players, falsified documents claiming a Timorese parent, 29 AFC matches forfeited, exclusion from the 2023 Asian Cup qualifiers, general secretary banned for 3 years — fine reported as US$20,000 by Goal and US$76,000 by Inside World Football, so no amount is given); nine passports later declared null and void; ASEAN Championship group stage 2004, 2018, 2020, 2024, 2026 (Wikipedia 'best result'); 2026 play-off: beat Brunei 6–1 on aggregate and joined Vietnam's group (VFF); U-22 beat the Philippines 3–0 at the 2023 SEA Games (MerahPutih headline). From memory, to check: FFTL founded 2002 (owner brief + Wikipedia), FIFA 12 Sep 2005 (as in tl.culture.sports_games), first official match March 2003 v Sri Lanka in Asian Cup qualifying (Wikipedia infobox, recalled). Women's team and futsal: only general statements (betexplorer listed women's qualifiers v Cambodia and Indonesia; an AFF page mentioned the futsal team's camp before the 2024 ASEAN Futsal Championship) — no women's history or futsal results given. fftl.tl did not appear in search results, so it is not cited. 2026 group-stage results not found: add them. Tetun: kualifikasaun, faze grupu, elejível, kamizola, pavillaun, sub-22/sub-23, Kopa Ázia. EN/ID added 2026-11-11: translated from the Portuguese and checked against the Tetun; same facts, numbers and names; names of clubs, competitions and athletes kept as written; British spelling in EN, EYD in ID, terms as in tl.culture.sports_games (FFTL = Football Federation of Timor-Leste / Federasi Sepak Bola Timor-Leste; IOC for COI; CPLP Games / Pesta Olahraga CPLP). 'Perdeu na secretaria' is rendered 'declared lost'. Checked: at the 2026 ASEAN Championship (ASEAN Hyundai Cup) Timor-Leste lost its Group A games 0–7 to Vietnam, 0–2 to Singapore and 0–3 to Indonesia and did not advance (Tatoli, 31 Jul 2026), so 'has not yet reached the semi-finals' still holds (Thailand was not in the 2026 group; the sentence is general). Search results say the 2nd play-off leg v Brunei was played in Kuala Lumpur because no Timorese stadium met the standards — not verified.
- **The Liga Futebol Timor-Leste and its clubs** (`tl.sports.football_league`, draft, 9 sources): Champions from the Wikipedia Primeira Divisão table and the RSSSF list (both in search results): 2016 SLB Laulara, 2017 Karketu Dili, 2018 Boavista, 2019 Lalenok United, 2020/2022/2024 not held, 2021, 2023, 2025 Karketu Dili, 2026 SLB Laulara — the 2026 title should be confirmed against a current source. 2020 Copa FFTL replaced the league (page exists; COVID-19 reason is our inference — check). Name change LFA → LFTL: inferred from Wikipedia season titles (2021 'Liga Futebol Amadora', 2025 'Liga Futebol Timor-Leste'); the exact year is not given. Lalenok United: first AFC continental berth for a Timorese club (Wikipedia). From memory, to check: Taça 12 de Novembro as the national cup and its link to 12 Nov 1991 (quiz question depends on it); LFA Super Taça format (league champion v cup winner); promotion/relegation between the two divisions; 'tournaments before 2016 but no regular league'. Tetun: divizaun, kampeaun, títulu, eliminatória, patrosinadór, manán-na'in. EN/ID added 2026-11-11: translated from the Portuguese and checked against the Tetun; same facts, numbers and names; names of clubs, competitions and athletes kept as written; British spelling in EN, EYD in ID, terms as in tl.culture.sports_games (FFTL = Football Federation of Timor-Leste / Federasi Sepak Bola Timor-Leste; IOC for COI; CPLP Games / Pesta Olahraga CPLP). Division names kept in Portuguese with glosses (Primeira/Segunda Divisão = First/Second Division); cup names kept as written (Taça 12 de Novembro with the gloss '12 November Cup', LFA Super Taça, Copa FFTL). Checked: AD SLB (Associação Desportiva Sport Laulara e Benfica) won the 2026 LFTL on 22 Jul 2026, ten years after its LFA 2016 title; the match was played at the Estádio Municipal de Díli (Tatoli, 22 Jul 2026).
- **Timor-Leste at the Olympic Games (2000–2024)** (`tl.sports.olympics`, draft, 9 sources): Checked in search results: four athletes under the IOC flag in Sydney 2000 (Olympedia); NOC recognised 3 Jul 2003 at the 115th IOC Session in Prague (Olympedia); flag bearers from the Wikipedia list (2004 Aguida Amaral; 2012 Augusto Ramos Soares opening / Juventina Napoleão closing; 2016 Francelina Cabral; 2020 Imelda Ximenes + Felisberto de Deus; 2024 Jolanio Guterres + Ana da Costa da Silva opening, Manuel Ataide + Ana da Costa da Silva closing) — no 2000 or 2008 entry was found, so none is given; Paris 2024 = 4 athletes (2 women, 2 men) in athletics, swimming and taekwondo (as in tl.culture.sports_games, Tatoli); Youth Olympics 2010 page exists. From memory, to check: names and sports of the four Sydney athletes (Martinho de Araújo weightlifting, Victor Ramos boxing, Calisto da Costa and Aguida Amaral marathon), the ovation at the Sydney opening ceremony, Athens 2004 = two athletes in athletics, Imelda Ximenes being a swimmer, 'universality places' for many TL athletes, and the opening-ceremony dates used in the timeline (15 Sep 2000, 13 Aug 2004, 23 Jul 2021, 26 Jul 2024). Sports of the Paris flag bearers were not confirmed, so they are not given. Tetun: porta-bandeira, serimónia abertura/enserramentu, universalidade, pódiu, halterofilista, boxeadór. EN/ID added 2026-11-11: translated from the Portuguese and checked against the Tetun; same facts, numbers and names; names of clubs, competitions and athletes kept as written; British spelling in EN, EYD in ID, terms as in tl.culture.sports_games (FFTL = Football Federation of Timor-Leste / Federasi Sepak Bola Timor-Leste; IOC for COI; CPLP Games / Pesta Olahraga CPLP). 'Universalidade' = 'universality' (ID 'universalitas'); 'Atletas Olímpicos Individuais' = 'Individual Olympic Athletes' / 'Atlet Olimpiade Perorangan' as in tl.culture.sports_games. No further facts were checked.
- **The Winter Olympic Games and the Paralympic Games** (`tl.sports.winter_paralympics`, draft, 14 sources): Checked in search results: Yohan born in France to a French father and Timorese mother; qualified in Serbia on 29 Dec 2013; first Timorese Winter Olympian at 19; 2014 slalom 43rd (Wikipedia says 43rd of 115 starters); flag bearer at opening and closing in 2014 and 2018; 2018 slalom DNF; 2022 giant slalom DNF and slalom 45th of 87; brother Alexi raced at FIS level until 2016. 2017 Asian Winter Games page exists (city Sapporo from memory). Paralympics: 2 Individual Paralympic Athletes in 2000; official debut Beijing 2008 with powerlifter Liliana (Lily) da Costa Silva, also flag bearer; London 2012 athletics; Rio 2016 two competitors; absent Tokyo 2020; Paris 2024 one athlete in athletics; never at Winter Paralympics; no Paralympic medal. ASEAN Para Games: one bronze in 2003 (Vietnam); 2023 (Cambodia) six medals incl. a gold by 'Teofilo' (Tatoli — full name not given). NOT checked: whether Timor-Leste took part in Milano Cortina 2026 (Feb 2026) — update if it did. Constitution art. 21 (citizens with disabilities) from memory. Tetun: neve, eskiadór, slalom jigante, levantamentu pezu, desportu adaptadu, maun-alin (brother). EN/ID added 2026-11-11: translated from the Portuguese and checked against the Tetun; same facts, numbers and names; names of clubs, competitions and athletes kept as written; British spelling in EN, EYD in ID, terms as in tl.culture.sports_games (FFTL = Football Federation of Timor-Leste / Federasi Sepak Bola Timor-Leste; IOC for COI; CPLP Games / Pesta Olahraga CPLP). Checked: Beijing 2022 giant slalom DNF (52nd after run 1, out in run 2) and slalom 45th (Olympedia and olympics.com results seen in search results). Milano Cortina 2026: no Timorese entry found (see tl.sports.overview) — confirm before keeping 'three times' and 'never at the Winter Paralympics'. The Paris 2024 Paralympian was Teófilo Freitas, athletics (Tatoli, 17 Aug 2024); the 2023 ASEAN Para Games gold medallist 'Teofilo' is probably the same athlete (Tatoli also links a 2023 Asian Para Games medal for him) — confirm before adding the surname. Tatoli names the committee 'Comité Paralimpic Nacional Timor-Leste (CPNTL)'. 'Powerlifting (halterofilismo adaptado)' = 'powerlifting (adapted weightlifting)' / 'powerlifting (angkat berat adaptif)'.
- **SEA Games, Asian Games and CPLP Games** (`tl.sports.sea_games`, draft, 15 sources): Checked in search results: first SEA Games 2003; first gold 2011 in kempo (Wikipedia names Julianto Pereira and Dorceyana Borgesenth — spelling odd, so no names given; the 2011 page lists only kempo medals); all-time tally on Wikipedia 3 gold, 10 silver, 43 bronze (it is unclear whether 2025 is included, so only '3 gold' is used); 2017 three bronze; 2019 two bronze (boxing, taekwondo); 2021 Games (Hanoi, May 2022) 0-3-2 with two silvers by Felisberto de Deus incl. the 10,000 m; 2023 eight bronze (Wikipedia and OCA); 2025 (Thailand, Dec) one silver, seven bronze and the sepak takraw debut. Wikipedia also calls 2013 the 'best performance' (not used). Asian Games: debut 2002 (page exists), Hangzhou 2022 (held 2023) no medals; participation in 2006, 2010, 2014 and 2018 is from memory/page titles (2010 and 2014 pages exist) — check 2006 and 2018. CPLP: XII Jogos Desportivos da CPLP in Dili, July 2025 (a Government resolution gives 17–27 July; the PM's closing speech is dated 26 July 2025), six countries (Portuguese mission report), Portugal 26 gold / 42 podiums (RTP/Lusa), >5,000 at the closing ceremony with Ramos-Horta and Xanana. Timor-Leste's own CPLP medal count was not found. Lusophony Games (2006, 2009, 2014) not covered: no source found. Tetun: multidesportivu, Jogus Desportivus CPLP nian, edisaun ba dala-12, marka prezensa. EN/ID added 2026-11-11: translated from the Portuguese and checked against the Tetun; same facts, numbers and names; names of clubs, competitions and athletes kept as written; British spelling in EN, EYD in ID, terms as in tl.culture.sports_games (FFTL = Football Federation of Timor-Leste / Federasi Sepak Bola Timor-Leste; IOC for COI; CPLP Games / Pesta Olahraga CPLP). ID keeps 'SEA Games' and 'Asian Games' as used in Indonesian media and writes 'Pesta Olahraga CPLP'. Checked: 2025 SEA Games (33rd, Thailand) = 8 medals: 1 silver (boxing, Elísio Gaio) and 7 bronze (4 boxing, 2 taekwondo, 1 women's e-sports, Mobile Legends) (Tatoli, 22 Dec 2025), as in the text.
- **Martial arts: pencak silat, kempo, taekwondo, karate and boxing** (`tl.sports.martial_arts`, draft, 13 sources): Sensitive topic — written neutrally; only PSHT, KORK and Kera Sakti are named, as in the sources. Checked in search results: Council of Ministers extraordinary meeting of 2 Jul 2013 decided the extinction of PSHT, KORK and Kerak/Kera Sakti with a ban on their activities, citing Martial Arts Law 10/2008 (The Diplomat describes the law itself as outlawing the groups — sources differ on the legal form); suspension of all martial-arts teaching and practice from Nov 2023 after deadly incidents (Macau Business: at least 4 dead), extended in Dec 2025 to June 2026 (AFP via search summary); Freedom House says martial arts would be more heavily regulated once the ban is lifted; Lei n.º 9/2026 de 22 de Julho, 'Regime jurídico do ensino, da aprendizagem e da prática de artes marciais' (Jornal da República, Série I, N.º 29 – only the table of contents was seen). NOT checked: whether the suspension ended in June 2026 or continues under the new law, whether Law 9/2026 repeals Law 10/2008, the full name 'Kmanek Oan Rai Klaran' for KORK, pencak silat arriving 'during the occupation', unemployment as a driver (general analysis), and judo/karate practice in Timor-Leste. Tetun: arte marsiál, baku-malu, dezempregu, reuniaun estraordinária, hakotu (extinguish), boxeadór. EN/ID added 2026-11-11: translated from the Portuguese and checked against the Tetun; same facts, numbers and names; names of clubs, competitions and athletes kept as written; British spelling in EN, EYD in ID, terms as in tl.culture.sports_games (FFTL = Football Federation of Timor-Leste / Federasi Sepak Bola Timor-Leste; IOC for COI; CPLP Games / Pesta Olahraga CPLP). 'Extinguir os grupos' = 'dissolve' (ID 'membubarkan'); laws as 'Law No.' / 'UU No.' as in other files. UPDATE NEEDED (Tetun/Portuguese not changed in this translation pass): on 3 Jun 2026 the Council of Ministers extended the suspension and the temporary closure of training places to 31 Dec 2026, until the new law is in force (Tatoli, 3 Jun 2026; this continues Res. 45/2023 of 10 Nov 2023, extended by Res. 17/2024, 60/2024, 22/2025 and 77/2025 of 17 Dec 2025, which matches 'December 2025 … until June 2026'). So facts[4] (year 2025) and section 4 should be brought up to date. Parliament passed the law in the final global vote on 6 Jul 2026 (39 for, 0 against, 20 abstentions; Tatoli). On 30 Oct 2026 the youth council CNJTL objected to the Government's plan to reactivate martial and ritual arts 'next year' (Tatoli). 2026-11-11 (main editor): updated after the translator's Tatoli check — suspension to 31 Dec 2026, Parliament vote 6 Jul 2026 and October 2026 plan (martial arts); 2026 marathon winners; MJDAC as the ministry for sport.
- **Athletics and the Dili Marathon** (`tl.sports.athletics_marathon`, draft, 12 sources): Checked in search results: 'Dili, City of Peace' campaign of President Ramos-Horta (mid-2009) behind both the Tour and the marathon; first marathon year unclear — a 2017 sponsor note says both events began in 2009, while a UN item of June 2011 is titled 'Second International Dili Marathon', suggesting 2010 (text says '2009 or 2010'); Nov 2024: Kenyan and Indonesian runners won the 21 km (Tatoli); Nov 2025: Tour and marathon scheduled for Aug 2026 (Tatoli); EU support for the 2026 marathon, theme forests and wildlife (EEAS); Felisberto de Deus: two silvers at the 2021 SEA Games (Hanoi, May 2022) incl. the 10,000 m — the other event was not confirmed (probably 5,000 m). NOT checked: whether the August 2026 edition took place; the full-marathon/short-race categories (only the 21 km is confirmed); 'after the 2006 crisis' as the motive; Calisto da Costa and Augusto Ramos Soares as runners (from memory); the marathon dates in the timeline (Sydney women's marathon 24 Sep 2000, Athens 22 Aug 2004, from memory). Section 4 is general sports education. Tetun: meia-maratona, pulmaun, salta naruk/aas, soe disku/pezu/dardu, pista. The EU theme is given in its English wording ('running for our forests and wildlife'). UPDATE NEEDED (not changed): the Dili International Marathon 2026 took place in early August 2026 — Felisberto de Deus (Tatoli: 'Felizberto') won the 21 km half marathon and Teófilo Freitas the para-athletics 10 km (Tatoli, 8 Aug 2026); facts[4] 'August 2026 (planned)' and section 3 can be updated. 2026-11-11 (main editor): updated after the translator's Tatoli check — suspension to 31 Dec 2026, Parliament vote 6 Jul 2026 and October 2026 plan (martial arts); 2026 marathon winners; MJDAC as the ministry for sport.
