## Society (8)

- **Population and the 2022 census** (`tl.society.population`, draft, 8 sources): Preliminary figures (1 340 434; 678 087 men; 662 347 women; 250 034 households; Dili ~324 000; Ataúro ~10 000; growth 1.8% vs 2.1%; doubling in 39 years) from the UNFPA/INETL preliminary results. Final total 1 341 737 appears in the INETL wall chart and in other search summaries — check against the main report. Median age 21.7 and dependency ratio 67.8 from the INETL wall chart (search summary, not read in full). Urban share 28.6% (2015: 29.5%). Census fieldwork began in September 2022 under the DGE (Ministry of Finance); INETL published the final report (May 2023) and thematic reports (14 May 2024). 2015 total ~1.18 million is rounded. Tetun: idade mediana, rácio dependénsia, baluk-haat ida (1/4), Diresaun-Jerál Estatístika.
- **Languages of Timor-Leste** (`tl.society.languages`, draft, 5 sources): 2015 census mother-tongue shares (Tetun Prasa 30.6, Mambai 16.6, Makasae 10.5, Tetun Terik 6.1, Baikeno 5.9; then Kemak, Bunak, Tokodede, Fataluku) come from a secondary list seen in search results — check against the INETL 2015 tables. 2022 census language tables were not found in this session. Wikipedia gives different 2010 shares (Tetun Prasa 36.6%), so we do not claim trends. Regional distribution of languages follows general linguistic sources (Wikipedia), not re-verified here. 'Around thirty languages and dialects' is an approximation (one source says 32). Tetun: orgullu, aproximadu, empresta.
- **Religion** (`tl.society.religion`, draft, 6 sources): Shares (97.6% Catholic, ~2% Protestant, ~0.2% Muslim) are 2015-census figures quoted by secondary sources (GCatholic, State Dept report, Tatoli); the official 2022 census religion table was not found — update if available. Articles 11(2), 12 and 45 checked in the local Tetun Konstituisaun. Papal visits: John Paul II at Tasitolu in 1989 (October; day not re-checked), Francis on 10 Sep 2024 with ~600 000 (organisers' estimate via the Vatican). Holiday list follows plan.json days. Growth of Catholicism under the occupation and the 'recognised religions' rule from general historical sources — check wording. Tetun: meskita, beiala, Korpu de Deus, Sesta-Feira Santa.
- **Education system and school levels** (`tl.society.education`, draft, 6 sources): Law No. 14/2008 of 29 Oct 2008 and its Article 8 (Tetun and Portuguese as languages of instruction) confirmed via search; the article numbers for the 9-year, 3-cycle structure, pre-school ages 3–5 and the two secondary paths follow the coordinator's description of the Ministry levels and our reading of the law — check against the full text (Planipolis PDF) and moe.gov.tl, which could not be opened in this session. 'Free' basic education: the Constitution says 'as far as possible, free'; we avoid saying it is fully free. Secondary areas (sciences & technology / social sciences & humanities), 'final exams' and class-teacher model in early years — check. UNTL created in 2000 from UNTIM and the Dili Polytechnic; faculty list from the 2014 brochure. Recurrent education/literacy: general wording.
- **Health** (`tl.society.health`, draft, 6 sources): Life expectancy: World Bank 2022 ≈ 67.4; Wikipedia (modelled) ≈ 69.9 — we give a range 67–70; the census mortality report has its own life tables (values not read). Infant mortality 45.6 (M 52.6, F 38.2) from the census mortality summary. Cuba: scholarships from 2004; >400 graduates in Dec 2012 (gov.tl); 1 054 doctors by 2021 (Tatoli) — WHO Bulletin gave ~700 by end 2013. Malaria: WHO certification 24 Jul 2025, >223 000 cases in 2006, zero local cases since 2021. HNGV as national referral hospital and referral hospitals in Baucau and Maliana from general knowledge — check. 'Very few doctors at independence' not quantified. Health advice in section 4 is general public-health guidance. Tetun: bee tasak (boiled water), sentina, sarampu, isin-rua, parteira, mear (cough), esperansa moris, mortalidade infantíl.
- **Cities and towns** (`tl.society.cities`, draft, 5 sources): Urban share 28.6% (2022) and 29.5% (2015) from the INETL wall chart/Tatoli via search summaries. Urban-area populations (Dili 267 623, Baucau 19 118, Pante Macassar 15 240, Maliana 13 078, Lospalos 12 782, Gleno 12 546, Suai 10 660) from citypopulation.de (census 2022); urban definitions differ between censuses. Baucau ~120 km from Dili, its limestone plateau, springs and long runway; Suai Supply Base; Vila Maumeta; RAEOA in 2014 — general knowledge and old Lafa data, not re-checked here. 'Mikrolet' and 'anggunas' are everyday transport words — check spelling (angguna).
- **Youth and women** (`tl.society.youth_women`, draft, 5 sources): Quota: one woman in every three candidates since the 2011 amendment (sources differ on the earlier one-in-four rule of 2007). Women ≈37% of MPs in mid-2023 (Freedom House 2024); exact number of women elected in 2023 not checked. First woman President of Parliament in 2023 (Freedom House: June 2023; name not used). Law 7/2010 'public crime', OPMT founded 1975, work abroad in Australia/South Korea/UK — general knowledge, not re-checked here. Arts. 17, 19 and 63 checked in the local Tetun Konstituisaun (Art. 17 quoted word for word). National Women's Day (day.women) is still marked 'verify' in plan.json, so no date is given. Tetun: pelumenus, gerrilleiru, mensajeira, fa'an-na'in, empreza-na'in, hato'o keixa.
- **Media and communication** (`tl.society.media`, draft, 6 sources): Art. 41 checked in the local Tetun Konstituisaun. Media Law: passed May 2014, Court of Appeal ruling 21 Aug 2014, final version enacted Nov 2014 (Amnesty/ETAN/Wikipedia summaries). Press Council, training and fact-checking: RSF. RSF 2026 rank 30/180 taken from a search summary of rsf.org — confirm the current figure. Tatoli: launched 2016 (sources give 27 July or 27 June 2016), publishes in Tetun, Portuguese, English and Indonesian. Newspaper list from RSF (RSF mentions Tetun, Portuguese and English). Radio Timor Kmanek (Catholic), community radios, TVTL reach and the three mobile operators (Timor Telecom, Telkomcel, Telemor) from general knowledge — check. Tetun: radiodifuzaun públiku, rona-na'in, Repórter Sein Fronteira, telemovel.

## Economy (13)

- **The economy at a glance** (`tl.economy.overview`, draft, 12 sources): GDP 2024 (US$1.87 bn) and GDP per person (US$1,332) from the World Bank country page and the WDI series on FRED (Dec 2025 vintage); non-oil GDP 2023 (US$1.802 bn) and non-oil growth 4% (2022) / 2.4% (2023) from IMF PR 24/479; 4.1% real growth 2024 from the World Bank MPO title. Dollar legal tender 24 Jan 2000 (UNTAET Reg. 2000/7) per Wikipedia; first centavos 10 Nov 2003 per BCTL. Checked: WTO membership on 30 Aug 2024 (166th member) and ASEAN membership on 26 Oct 2025 (11th member, 47th Summit in Kuala Lumpur). 'Very few jobs from oil' follows IMF/Australian Treasury analyses (offshore fields, little local employment). Tetun to check: PIB naun-petrolíferu, kresimentu reál, OMC (Organizasaun Mundiál Komérsiu), Estadus Unidus, taxa juru.
- **Oil and gas** (`tl.economy.petroleum`, draft, 14 sources): Checked in search results: Bayu-Undan discovered 1995, liquids from April 2004, sales gas/LNG from 2006, gas to Darwin ended late 2023 (last cargo 11 Nov 2023), production ceased 4 June 2025 (Tatoli; Rigzone said May 2025); Kitan: Eni 40%, Inpex 35%, Talisman 25%, first oil Oct 2011, ended 2015 (earlier forecasts said 2016); Greater Sunrise purchase completed 16 Apr 2019 (ConocoPhillips 30% for US$350m, Shell 26.56% for US$300m), TIMOR GAP 56.56%, Woodside 33.44%, Osaka Gas 10%. Tasi Mane checked: three clusters along about 155 km from Suai (Covalima) to Beaço (Viqueque) linked by a highway (2011 plan); reorganised in 2023 (per TIMOR GAP, in a search summary) into the Suai Supply Base and a combined refinery/LNG hub at Natarbora; consortium has preferred Darwin processing (Lowy Interpreter). Not re-checked: Greater Sunrise found in the 1970s (often given as 1974), the 2018 treaty year, and what exactly has been built so far. Tetun to check: kanu-gás (pipeline), posu (well), kondensadu, gás ba te'in (LPG), Beasu as Tetun spelling of Beaço.
- **The Petroleum Fund** (`tl.economy.petroleum_fund`, draft, 11 sources): Law passed 22 Jun 2005, published 3 Aug 2005 (IFSWF self-assessment); opening balance US$205 m and 2019 transparency rank (7th of 64, Peterson Institute) from Wikipedia; ESI = 3% of petroleum wealth (BCTL, La'o Hamutuk, IFSWF). Balances: 18.29 bn end-2023 and 18.45 bn Q1 2024 (Tatoli/BCTL), 18.61 bn end-2025, 18.43 bn on 30 Jun 2026; cumulative receipts 25.30 bn, investment returns 12.65 bn, withdrawals 19.66 bn and US$600 m Q2 transfer from the BCTL Q2-2026 report via Tatoli. End-2024 balance (about 18.27 bn) only in a secondary report, so left out. 'Since about 2009' above the ESI from East Asia Forum/Böll. 'Bonds and shares' is a simplification of the investment mandate – check. The World Bank 'Bulk' document title was not visible in search; replace with its proper title. Tetun to check: RSE (Rendimentu Sustentável Estimadu), riku-soin petrolíferu, obrigasaun no asaun, Konsellu Konsultivu, fundu soberanu.
- **Exports** (`tl.economy.exports`, draft, 11 sources): Checked: WTO member 30 Aug 2024, ASEAN member 26 Oct 2025; Bayu-Undan liquids 2004, LNG via Darwin 2006–2023, production end June 2025; coffee exports US$15.4 m (2023) and US$17.7 m (2024) and total goods exports US$73.2 m (2024) in the INETL 2024 trade publication; BCTL (via Lusa, March 2025): non-oil exports +21.7% to about US$21 m in 2024, coffee the main item, Indonesia and the United States the main destinations (INETL and BCTL totals differ – definitions). Not checked: 'almost all export earnings from petroleum', LNG buyers 'mainly in Asia', candlenut and vanilla as the next exports. Portuguese name for candlenut ('noz-de-iguape') to confirm. Tetun: ró-tanke, alfándega, kolleita.
- **Imports** (`tl.economy.imports`, draft, 8 sources): Checked: goods imports US$953.6 m and exports US$73.2 m in 2024 (INETL 2024 trade publication; deficit about US$880 m computed); BCTL via Lusa: imports +12.5% to US$923.2 m in 2024 with Indonesia and China the main suppliers (totals differ by definition). One secondary source puts Indonesia at about 70% of imports (no year) – not used. Still to verify (search budget was limited): the list of import products, Singapore/Malaysia as fuel sources, rice suppliers, how the trade gap is financed (Petroleum Fund withdrawals, aid, remittances), that Tibar replaced Dili port for containers. Tetun: masin-midar, mina-te'in, gazóleu/gazolina, na'an jeladu, ró-kontentór, défisit komérsiu.
- **Coffee and the Timor Hybrid** (`tl.economy.coffee`, draft, 10 sources): Checked: Timor Hybrid found in 1927 in a Typica plantation on Timor (one Portuguese source says Ermera); CIFC (Portugal) received seed in the late 1950s (1957–59 by source) and bred Catimor (× Caturra) and Sarchimor (× Villa Sarchi); rust reached Brazil in 1970; CCT: 1994 USAID-funded NCBA project organising 450 farmers, now 20,000+ (Royal Coffee) or about 27,000 (NCBA) member families, organic/Fairtrade arabica, health clinics; coffee exports US$15.4 m (2023) and US$17.7 m (2024, INETL). Still to check: coffee areas (Ermera with Letefoho, Railaco, Hatulia; Liquiçá, Aileu, Ainaro, Manufahi, Bobonaro), harvest in the dry season, coffee introduced by the Portuguese in the 19th century (often 1815), forced planting in colonial times, military-linked control of the trade under Indonesian occupation. Better sources to add: World Coffee Research variety pages, MAF. Tetun to check: Híbridu Timór, ferruzen kafé nian, kafé fuan mean, tesi (prune), kolleita, rihun sanulu barak.
- **Agriculture and food crops** (`tl.economy.agriculture`, draft, 7 sources): Checked: 66% of households have agricultural holdings (141,141 of 213,417; 2019 census factsheet and Tatoli – one later Tatoli article says 60%); Bobonaro has the largest farm area (Tatoli, Oct 2020). The rest is general and comes from well-known facts and the old Lafa atlas (search budget was limited). Rice plains (Maliana, Uatolari/Uatucarbau, Natarbora, Suai–Betano) and highland vegetables (Maubisse, Hatubuilico) come from the old Lafa atlas. Check the length of the rainy season (north vs south), the 'hungry season', El Niño effects and the support programmes; add MAF fact-file and 2019 agriculture census links and a share of farming households with a year. Tetun to check: Uatukarbau (Tetun spelling of Uatucarbau), repollu, fehuk-malae, fore-mungu, fore-rai, talas, sabraka, enxada, tempu hamlaha, ku'u.
- **Livestock and fisheries** (`tl.economy.livestock_fisheries`, draft, 8 sources): Checked: ASF first outbreak in Dili on 9 Sep 2019 (confirmed 26 Sep), about 16,000 pigs dead by Dec 2019 per MAF (official reports only, true toll likely higher), herd of about 419,000 pigs in the 2015 census. The rest is general (search budget was limited). Check in particular: buffalo trampling paddies, Bali cattle sold for meat, lower fish consumption than neighbours (FAO), illegal foreign fishing, tara bandu used for fisheries. Add livestock numbers with a year (2015 census / 2019 agriculture census) and a fisheries source (FAO / Ministry). Tetun to check: karau = buffalo, karau-baka = cattle (the old atlas used karau-baka for buffalo), bero (canoe), bubu (fish trap), sama natar, hahán, peste suína afrikana, lekirauk.
- **Natural resources and minerals** (`tl.economy.natural_resources`, draft, 8 sources): Article 139 summarised from the Tetun text of the Konstituisaun; petroleum facts checked (see tl.economy.petroleum). Checked in Chambers Mining 2025/2026 guides: minerals listed in the ESCAP atlas (chromite, copper, gold, silver, manganese; limestone, marble), Mining Code Law 12/2021 of 30 June 2021, March 2023 tender of 49 areas and November 2023 prospecting licences for 13 areas; Estrella's manganese leases in Baucau and its venture with Murak Rai Timor (La'o Hamutuk, 2024). The petroleum regulator is left unnamed on purpose: ANPM was split in 2023–2024 and mining now has its own regulator (ANM); confirm current names before adding them. The 'uplifted sea-floor rocks' geology line and river-mining rules are general knowledge – check with tl.land.geology. Tetun to check: fatuk-ahu (limestone), ahu (lime), manganés, osan-mean (gold), bee-sa'e (flood), ai-kameli (sandalwood).
- **Infrastructure: roads, ports, airports, power** (`tl.economy.infrastructure`, draft, 13 sources): Checked: Tibar 30-year concession awarded to Bolloré in June 2016, first PPP, about US$490 m in total (split between Bolloré and the State varies by source), financial close Aug 2018, first stage completed 2022, now run by Africa Global Logistics (MSC bought Bolloré Africa Logistics in late 2022); Hera 119 MW, Betano 136 MW, both in full operation since 2012 (Hera first operation Nov 2011/early 2012), 150 kV loop of 603 circuit-km, 99.9% diesel (UNEP work plan, undated), plants designed for later gas use; airport runway 1,850 m, groundbreaking 20 May 2025, works by Waskita Karya from Oct 2025, about US$239 m, completion by 2028, co-financed by ADB (US$135 m loan, 2021), JICA and Australia (AIFFP). NOT checked: road programmes and partners (ADB, World Bank, Japan), 'before 2010 power only in Dili and a few towns', solar studies, water/internet plans; no opening date for Tibar found. Tetun to check: rai-halai (landslide), rai-rahun (dirt road), mina-rai todan (heavy fuel oil), parseria públiku-privada, hanaruk pista.
- **Central Bank and finance** (`tl.economy.banking`, draft, 9 sources): Checked: BCTL established 13 Sep 2011 under Law 5/2011 (Constitution art. 143); predecessors Central Payments Office (2000–2001) and Banking & Payments Authority (Nov 2001–2011) with the roles given; five commercial banks (BNCTL – only State-owned; BNU Timor – majority CGD; ANZ, Bank Mandiri and BRI branches) per BCTL list and Wikipedia (2021 data); June 2025: assets US$2,478 m, credit US$640 m, NPL 2.4%, deposits US$1,735 m. The Asian Banker says BNCTL makes about 75% of all lending – written as 'a large part'. Check that ANZ still operates (Wikipedia list dated 2021). Not checked: 'banking had to start again almost from nothing after 1999', financial-inclusion work, mobile money agents, microfinance and savings groups. Tetun to check: Ofísiu Sentrál Pagamentu, Autoridade Bankária no Pagamentu, kámara kompensasaun, sukursál, selu fila, juru.
- **Strategic Development Plan 2011–2030** (`tl.economy.development_plan`, draft, 7 sources): Checked: vision (low to upper-middle income by 2030, healthy, well-educated, safe population), the three pillars and their sub-areas, the stages 2011–2015 / 2016–2020 / 2021–2030, national consultations launched by PM Xanana Gusmão in April 2010, Council of Ministers extraordinary meeting on 30 June 2011. NOT confirmed: a formal vote of the National Parliament on the SDP (often said to be July 2011) – so the text only says it was 'launched' in 2011; the line on the plan's institutional and macro-economic chapters; 'more schools and clinics'; that later Governments use it as their main guide. Tetun to check: PED (Planu Estratéjiku Dezenvolvimentu), kapitál sosiál, rendimentu médiu-aas, isin-di'ak, esbosu, hamriik iha pilár.
- **Jobs and workers abroad** (`tl.economy.jobs_migration`, draft, 8 sources): Checked: LFS 2021 – informal employment 77.3% (women 80.4%, men 75.3%), unemployment 5.1%, youth (15–24) 9.6% (ILO; one Tatoli piece wrongly calls 9.6% the national rate); Government employs about 8 in 10 formal workers (Tatoli 2026); Korea programme since 2009, Australia since 2012; Q1 2026: 1,071 to Australia, 309 to Korea; 2024 remittances US$244.8 m (UK 100, Australia 61, Korea 44.8, others 39 – BCTL Governor via Tatoli). The BCTL Annual Economic Performance Report 2025 gives US$211.6 m for 2024 and US$181.9 m for 2025 (probably a net/balance measure) – reviewer should pick one series. NOT checked: kinds of jobs in Korea, Australia and the UK, Portuguese passports as the route to the UK, Timorese communities in English towns, the NEET share (search summary was inconsistent), 'one of the most important sources of foreign income'. Tetun to check: Peskiza Forsa Traballu, servisu informál, dezempregu, mikrolete, traballadór sazonál, remesa, Sistema Autorizasaun Empregu, armazén.

## Tourism (123)

- **Tourism in Timor-Leste** (`tl.tourism.overview`, draft, 7 sources): Written on 27 Oct 2026; part of the research was done after the shared web-search budget ran out, so some facts come from general knowledge and must be checked against the cited pages. Visa rules are described only in general terms; check with the Migration Service before adding details. Check: the WHO malaria-free certification (2025), travel times (Dili–Baucau about 3 h, Dili–Maubisse 2–3 h), the 2027 'underrated destinations' list (title of a timorleste.tl news item; the list's publisher was not checked) and the number of municipalities (14, counting Ataúro and the RAEOA). Tetun terms to check: 'ai-moruk susuk nian' (repellent), 'bee nono' (boiled water), 'responsavel'.
- **Diving and marine tourism** (`tl.tourism.diving_marine`, draft, 7 sources): Written on 27 Oct 2026; part of the research was done after the shared web-search budget ran out, so some facts come from general knowledge and must be checked against the cited pages. Check: whale species and season (about October–November is commonly given for Dili), the 'six countries' of the Coral Triangle Initiative (CTI-CFF), and community dive fees on Ataúro (they vary by village). Tetun 'raia' (ray, from Portuguese) and 'bóia' to be checked.
- **Mountains and trekking** (`tl.tourism.mountains_trekking`, draft, 8 sources): Written on 27 Oct 2026; part of the research was done after the shared web-search budget ran out, so some facts come from general knowledge and must be checked against the cited pages. Heights differ between sources: Tatamailau 2,963 or 2,986 m; Matebian 2,315 or 2,376 m; Maubohilau 2,312 m (timorleste.tl calls it the third-highest peak, which conflicts with 2,315 m for Matebian — check). The 'Five Summits' list itself was not seen; check which peaks it includes. Bandeira waterfall 'said to be the highest in Timor-Leste' (timorleste.tl, Ermera). Climb times are estimates from visitor reports.
- **History and heritage places** (`tl.tourism.heritage`, draft, 9 sources): Written on 27 Oct 2026; part of the research was done after the shared web-search budget ran out, so some facts come from general knowledge and must be checked against the cited pages. Check: the Jerimalai date (about 42,000 years) and fish species; the 1515 date (traditional); the dates of the forts; the Suai church massacre date (6 September 1999). Sensitive history is summarised briefly and victim numbers are not given where sources differ.
- **Community and eco-tourism** (`tl.tourism.community_eco`, draft, 8 sources): Written on 27 Oct 2026; part of the research was done after the shared web-search budget ran out, so some facts come from general knowledge and must be checked against the cited pages. Constitution, Article 2(4), checked against the Tetun text (konstituisaun.txt). Check the list of community stays (homestays change often) and the Boneca de Ataúro group. The statement on rubbish collection is general.
- **Markets and local food for visitors** (`tl.tourism.food_markets`, draft, 7 sources): Written on 27 Oct 2026; part of the research was done after the shared web-search budget ran out, so some facts come from general knowledge and must be checked against the cited pages. Market days (Aileu: Wednesday and Saturday; Lospalos: Friday and Saturday) from timorleste.tl. Check the dish descriptions (batar da'an, katupa), the drinks tua mutin and tua sabu, and Tetun food words (lakeru = pumpkin, sukaer = tamarind, ai-dila = papaya, sabraka = orange). The coffee regions follow the old Lafa atlas. EN/ID (2026-11-11): translated from the Portuguese with the terms of the other tourism articles, places.json and tl.culture.food (Tais Market / Pasar Tais, centavo coins / koin sentavo, Timor Hybrid / Hibrida Timor, areca nut and betel leaf / pinang dan sirih, tua mutin = palm wine / tuak).
- **Festivals and events** (`tl.tourism.events_festivals`, draft, 7 sources): Written on 2 Nov 2026 from WebSearch result summaries (timorleste.tl itself could not be opened in the sandbox). 2026 dates (Tasifest 29–30 May; Fatubessi pilgrimage 22 Aug; Dili International Film Festival 24 Sep–12 Oct; Aileu Coffee Festival 27 Sep, though the homepage also listed 12, 26 and 27 Sep; Timor-Leste Coffee Festival in Dili in November, date TBC; Dili International Marathon listed for 8 Aug (TBC) with a national selection race on 9 May; Tour de Timor revival planned for the second week of August, starting from Indonesia; Saturday night market at Timor Plaza 3–9 pm) come from timorleste.tl/events and Tatoli (8 May 2026) as summarised — check whether the Tour and the Marathon actually took place. The older name 'Dili City of Peace Marathon' was not confirmed and is not used. A Dili Carnival (early 2010s) and a national 'Festival of the Arts' were asked for but could not be confirmed, so they are left out. Tour de Timor details follow culture_more.json (tl.culture.sports_games). Pope Francis Mass (about 600,000 people) as in other files (NCR/UPI). Whale season wording reconciles 'October–November' (tourism articles) and 'October–December' (tl.nature.marine). Tetun 'muslin', 'funerál', 'simeira' to check. English and Indonesian translated from the Portuguese on 11 Nov 2026 (no facts added or changed; Tetun and Portuguese untouched). 'Natal do Povo' kept in Portuguese with a translation ('People's Christmas' / 'Natal Rakyat') — check whether the Presidency uses an official English name. All dates copied exactly from the Portuguese.
- **Practical information for visitors** (`tl.tourism.practical_info`, draft, 9 sources): Written on 2 Nov 2026 from WebSearch result summaries (timorleste.tl itself could not be opened in the sandbox). Visa rules (visa on arrival US$30 in cash; proof of accommodation, funds and a return ticket; e-Declaration 'up to 5 days before departure', written here as 'in the 5 days before departure' — check the exact window) from timorleste.tl/plan-your-trip as summarised; '30 days' and extension fees come from third-party visa sites, which disagree (about US$1 per day vs US$35 per 30 days), so no fee for extensions is given. Exemptions disagree between sources (official site: UN/Interpol passports; third-party: Schengen/EU and Indonesian citizens 90 days) — the text only says 'some nationalities have their own rules'; rules for ASEAN citizens were not found. Ferry details (Berlin Nakroma Wed/Thu/Sat/Sun 08:00, return 15:00, US$4; the boat 'Success' from Kolmera Tue/Fri, US$5; Berlin Ramelau Tue/Fri 17:00, about 12 h, US$8) from timorleste.tl/plan-your-trip/ferries (APORTIL timetable 2026) as summarised; the 'Dragon Star' fast boat mentioned in the brief was not found. Taxi meters, SIM registration with a passport and the Guido Valadares hospital are general knowledge — check. Currency facts follow tl.symbols.currency (URLs for coins and WHO verified by other writers). English and Indonesian translated from the Portuguese on 11 Nov 2026 (no facts added or changed; Tetun and Portuguese untouched). Ferry times '8 horas' / '15 horas' given as 8 a.m. / 3 p.m. (en) and pukul 08.00 / 15.00 (id); prices and periods copied exactly. 'Serviço de Migração' given as 'Migration Service' / 'Dinas Migrasi' with the Portuguese name in brackets — check the official English name. WHO keeps its official spelling 'World Health Organization'.
- **Beaches and islands** (`tl.tourism.beaches_islands`, draft, 8 sources): Written on 2 Nov 2026 from WebSearch result summaries (timorleste.tl itself could not be opened in the sandbox). Summarises existing places plus the new ones in places_more.json. Ferry durations from timorleste.tl/plan-your-trip/ferries (search summary, 2026 timetable). Ataúro area (~140 km²), Manucoco (~1000 m) and the 2016 reef-fish study as in other Lafa files (Mongabay and Wikipedia URLs verified by other writers). Natarbora's beaches as in municipalities.json. Jaco: no overnight stays, following place.jaco_island. Tetun 'kór naroman' (light-coloured) and 'matak-azúl' (turquoise) to check. English and Indonesian translated from the Portuguese on 11 Nov 2026 (no facts added or changed; Tetun and Portuguese untouched).
- **Coffee land and mountain villages** (`tl.tourism.coffee_villages`, draft, 6 sources): Written on 2 Nov 2026 from WebSearch result summaries (timorleste.tl itself could not be opened in the sandbox). Harvest May–September, Maubisse farms including CCT, the advice on guides/4×4 and guesthouses in Maubisse, Aileu and Ermera from timorleste.tl 'A Coffee Lover's Pilgrimage' (search summary); the Letefoho farm visits from timorleste.tl 'A Trip To Coffee Country' (run with Café Brisa Serena according to a coffee trade listing; the group is not named in the text). Letefoho harvest June–September from a trade listing. Timor Hybrid (1927) and CCT (1994; 20,000+ families) follow tl.economy.coffee. Coffee festivals as in tl.tourism.events_festivals. Tetun 'sona' (roast) and 'lona' (tarpaulin) to check. English and Indonesian translated from the Portuguese on 11 Nov 2026 (no facts added or changed; Tetun and Portuguese untouched). 'Cooperativa Café Timor' kept in Portuguese (the cooperative's own name).
- **Cristo Rei of Dili** (`place.cristo_rei`, draft, 5 sources): Written on 27 Oct 2026 after the shared web-search budget ran out: facts come from the cited pages as far as seen in search results plus general knowledge; coordinates are approximate (recalled or estimated, checked only against the Lafa municipality map) — verify on OpenStreetMap/Wikipedia. Coordinates from English Wikipedia (8°31′14″S 125°36′30″E). Check: inauguration year 1996 and the '27th province' meaning of the height (Wikipedia); the number of steps (sources say from about 500 to 570). A 2024 review said the top part was closed for a while; check access.

## Holidays and national days (22)

- **Public holidays and commemorative dates** (`tl.calendar.overview`, draft, 8 sources): 18 holidays assumes Law n.º 10/2023 (5 April 2023) made 3 November a holiday. Lusa/dnoticias (13 Mar 2023) reported Parliament's approval, and 2024–2025 despachos (JR Série II) cite 'Leis n.os 3/2016 e 10/2023'. I could not read the law itself, so confirm it. The 17 holidays before 2023 match the 2017 government list a)–q). 'UNTAET executive order 2000' comes from a search snippet (Executive Order 2000/1?). Article references (despacho of the labour minister for movable dates; PM grants tolerância de ponto under art. 7) come from search snippets of the government notices. Tetun: 'toleransia pontu' as in the government's Tetun notices; 'despaxu' needs a check.
- **Religious holidays in the national calendar** (`tl.calendar.liturgical`, draft, 8 sources): Constitution arts. 11, 12 and 45 checked against the Tetun text. From memory, so please check: concordat with the Holy See signed in 2015 (14 Aug 2015?); John Paul II visit Oct 1989 (12 Oct); Francis 9–11 Sept 2024 with the Tasitolu Mass on 10 Sept; Dili cathedral dedicated to the Immaculate Conception. Idul Adha 22 Aug 2018 and 11 Aug 2019 are from government notices. Tetun liturgical terms to check: Adventu, Kuaresma, Kuarta-Feira Sinzas, Tempu Komún, konkordata, Santa Sé, fulan-kabuar, fulan-foun.
- **New Year's Day** (`day.new_year`, draft, 3 sources): Fixed-date holiday a) in the 2017 government list. The Mass of Mary, Mother of God, on 1 January and the midnight fireworks in Dili are general knowledge. Tetun: 'fogu-de-artifísiu'.
- **Veterans' Day** (`day.veterans`, draft, 5 sources): Holiday since Law n.º 3/2016 (2017 government list item b). NOT VERIFIED: the link between 3 March and the March 1981 national conference at Lacluta (Mabai), where Xanana Gusmão was chosen as leader. I wrote it as 'many people link the date to'; check the preamble of Law 3/2016. The State veteran register, pensions and medals (Estatuto dos Combatentes da Libertação Nacional) are from memory. Constitution art. 11 checked in the Tetun text.
- **Good Friday** (`day.good_friday`, draft, 4 sources): Movable holiday (Easter − 2 days), fixed each year by despacho; 2017 list item c. Easter 2026 = 5 April (Good Friday 3 April); Easter 2027 = 28 March (Good Friday 26 March). The Stations of the Cross on the Cristo Rei path are from memory. Tetun: 'Via-Sakra', 'Domingu Ramus', 'Na'i nia Paixaun'.
- **International Workers' Day** (`day.labour`, draft, 4 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item d, 'Dia Mundial do Trabalhador'). Constitution art. 50 checked in the Tetun text. Chicago 1886 and the 1889 Second International are standard history. Tetun: 'traballadór', 'selu-kole' (wages, as in the Constitution), 'marxa'.
- **Restoration of Independence Day** (`day.restoration`, draft, 5 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item e). Constitution facts (22 Mar 2002 approval, in force 20 May 2002, Constituent Assembly elected 30 Aug 2001) checked in the Tetun text. Tasitolu ceremony with Kofi Annan, UN membership on 27 Sep 2002 and presidential inaugurations on 20 May (2007, 2012, 2017, 2022) are well known. Check the list of foreign guests if more are added.
- **Corpus Christi** (`day.corpus_christi`, draft, 3 sources): Movable holiday (Easter + 60 days, Thursday), fixed yearly by despacho; the 2017 list had it on 15 June 2017 (Easter 16 April 2017 + 60). The feast was established in the 13th century (1264). Tetun: 'ostensóriu', 'lalatak' (canopy), 'Primeira Komuñaun'.
- **Popular Consultation Day** (`day.consultation`, draft, 5 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item h). Ballot facts (5 May 1999 agreements, UNAMET, turnout ≈98%, 78.5% against autonomy, result announced 4 Sep 1999) are widely documented; ≈250 000 displaced is the usual UNHCR figure; INTERFET arrived 20 Sep 1999. The Constituent Assembly election of 30 Aug 2001 is in the Constitution's preamble. The maritime treaty entered into force on 30 Aug 2019.
- **All Saints' Day** (`day.all_saints`, draft, 3 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item j). The customs (cleaning graves, candles at Santa Cruz and other cemeteries, travel home) are widely observed; I have no written source for them.
- **All Souls' Day** (`day.all_souls`, draft, 4 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item k, 'Dia de Todos os Fiéis Defuntos'). The link with ancestor respect (matebian, uma lulik) is general knowledge and should be phrased with care by a native reviewer. The three days in a row since 2023 assume that 3 November is a holiday (see day.women).
- **National Youth Day** (`day.youth`, draft, 4 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 Tetun list: 'Loron Nasionál Foin-Sa'e sira nian'). Death toll given cautiously ('more than 250 died', as in human rights estimates such as 271 dead; others missing); check against the Santa Cruz article by writer C. Sebastião Gomes (28 Oct 1991) and Max Stahl are well documented. Constitution art. 19 checked.
- **Proclamation of Independence Day** (`day.proclamation`, draft, 5 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item m). Constitution art. 1(2) and the preamble were checked in the Tetun text. Anthem 'Pátria' with words by Borja da Costa, first sung at the proclamation, is from the old Lafa data. 50th anniversary in 2025: no details given.
- **Memorial Day** (`day.memorial`, draft, 5 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item n, 'Dia da Memória'). CAVR figures (at least 102 800 conflict-related deaths; ≈18 600 killings and ≈84 200 hunger/illness deaths): check against Chega!. 'Until 2016 called National Heroes Day' is inferred from search snippets about Law 3/2016 and the 2016 calendar; verify it in the law text. Borja da Costa killed 7 Dec 1975 (old Lafa data). Preamble wording checked.
- **Immaculate Conception** (`day.immaculate`, draft, 4 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item o). From memory: the dogma of 1854, Portugal's patroness (1646, '17th century'), and Dili's cathedral of the Immaculate Conception as the seat of the Archbishop (Dili became an archdiocese in 2019). Tetun: 'padroeira', 'solenidade', 'rozáriu'.
- **Christmas Day** (`day.christmas`, draft, 3 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item p). Customs (presépios, Midnight Mass, carols, travel home) are general knowledge for Timor-Leste. Tetun: 'presépiu', 'Misa kalan-boot', 'luhan' (stable).
- **National Heroes Day** (`day.heroes`, draft, 6 sources): Fixed-date holiday (2017 list item q); search snippets say a 2016 act fixed 31 December in memory of Nicolau Lobato. 'Before 2016 heroes were honoured on 7 December' is inferred; verify. Wikipedia: Lobato born 24 May 1946 in Soibada, killed 31 Dec 1978. His role after Amaral's removal in 1977 is from memory. Constitution art. 11(4) checked.
- **Idul Fitri (Eid al-Fitr)** (`day.eid_fitr`, draft, 4 sources): Movable holiday (Islamic calendar; 2017 list item g: 26 June 2017). Dates in 'islamic_dates' are ASTRONOMICAL ESTIMATES (Meeus new moons + crescent-visibility criteria; the web-search budget ran out). 2024-04-10 and 2025-03-31 are the Indonesian dates (Saudi 30 Mar 2025). 2026 was a borderline moon: Saudi 20 Mar, while the MABIMS criterion suggests 21 Mar. 2027–2030 use the regional criterion, and Saudi Umm al-Qura dates are often one day earlier (2027-03-09, 2028-02-26, 2029-02-14). Check each year's despacho. The An-Nur mosque in Kampung Alor is from memory.
- **Idul Adha (Eid al-Adha)** (`day.eid_adha`, draft, 4 sources): Movable holiday (Islamic calendar). Government notices: 1 Sep 2017 (2017 list), 22 Aug 2018 and 11 Aug 2019 (despacho on the Islamic Community Centre's advice). The 2024–2030 dates in 'islamic_dates' are astronomical estimates: 2024-06-17 (Indonesia; Saudi 16 Jun) and 2025-06-06. For 2027 and later, Saudi dates may be one day earlier (2027-05-16). Check each year's despacho.
- **FALINTIL Day** (`day.falintil`, draft, 5 sources): Commemorative date (not a holiday): it is not in the 2017 or 2021 holiday lists, and government notices granted tolerância de ponto for 20 August in 2010, 2013, 2014, 2015 and 2020 (the 2020 notice cites the law as amended in 2016). From memory: FALINTIL founded 20 Aug 1975, declared non-party in 1987, F-FDTL created on 1 Feb 2001. 50th anniversary in 2025: no details given.
- **National Women's Day** (`day.women`, draft, 8 sources): TYPE: written as 'holiday' because Lusa/dnoticias (13 Mar 2023) reported that Parliament added 3 November to the national holidays. 2024–2025 despachos cite 'Lei n.º 10/2005 … alterada pelas Leis n.os 3/2016 … e 10/2023, de 5 de abril', and Calendarific and Office Holidays list it as a national holiday in 2026. I could not read Law 10/2023 itself, so please confirm it. The plan called it 'verify'. Maria Tapó (died 3 Nov 1975), Lobato's 1976 declaration and the 1977 FRETILIN resolution come from Tatoli. The 2026 50th-anniversary programme (Bobonaro 3 Nov, Dili 6 Nov) comes from Tatoli, 26 Aug 2026. Constitution art. 17 checked.
- **International Children's Day** (`day.children`, draft, 4 sources): TYPE: 'commemorative'. It is not in the government holiday lists (2017, 2021). I could NOT confirm whether 1 June is among the law's 'datas oficiais comemorativas' (the web-search budget ran out). It is widely marked in schools as an international day; if it is not official, keep it as an international observance. Constitution art. 18 checked. Timor-Leste acceded to the CRC in 2003 (from memory). The share of children in the population is left vague on purpose.

## Timor-Leste in the world (10)

- **Foreign policy of Timor-Leste** (`tl.world.overview`, draft, 7 sources): Verified in searches: UN admission 27 Sept 2002 (191st), ASEAN Regional Forum July 2005, ASEAN membership 26 Oct 2025, Ramos-Horta's UN posts (2013–2014 Guinea-Bissau; 2014–2015 peace-operations panel). Other memberships and dates (CPLP 2002, WTO 30 Aug 2024, g7+ 2010) are covered in the specific articles. The list of partners is general. Check the current official name of the Ministry (Negócios Estrangeiros e Cooperação). Tetun: 'relasaun privilejiada', 'karta kredensiál', 'Organizasaun Mundiál Komérsiu (OMC)', 'Koreia-Súl'.
- **Timor-Leste and the United Nations** (`tl.world.united_nations`, draft, 7 sources): Verified in searches: SC Res. 1414 (23 May 2002), GA admission 27 Sept 2002 without a vote (A/RES/57/3, 191st member), mission names and dates, UNMIT end on 31 Dec 2012, police handover 27 March 2011, Ramos-Horta's UN roles. From memory: 1960 listing as a non-self-governing territory; SC Res. 384 of 22 Dec 1975; local UNAMET staff killed in 1999; INTERFET authorised in September 1999 (Res. 1264). Tetun: 'Nasoens Unidas' (Constitution spelling), 'autodeterminasaun', 'Administradór Tranzitóriu', 'Objetivu Dezenvolvimentu Sustentável'.
- **CPLP: the Portuguese-speaking community** (`tl.world.cplp`, draft, 6 sources): Verified in searches: Dili summit 23 July 2014 (10th Conference), Equatorial Guinea admitted as 9th member (death-penalty debate), Taur Matan Ruak chair 2014–2016 (Government release), Zacarias da Costa Executive Secretary 2021–2025 (Ver Angola). From memory: CPLP founded 17 July 1996 in Lisbon by seven countries; Timor-Leste joined in 2002 (sources give 20 May or the Brasília summit of 31 July–1 August 2002 — exact day left out); 'nearly 300 million' people; teachers and judges from Portugal and Brazil; 'first CPLP summit in Asia' (Timor-Leste is the only Asian member). Tetun: 'simeira', 'membru daualu/dasia', 'Giné Ekuatoriál', 'Sau Tomé no Prínsipe'.
- **ASEAN: from observer to member (2025)** (`tl.world.asean`, draft, 6 sources): Verified in searches: admission on 26 Oct 2025 at the 47th Summit in Kuala Lumpur (Declaration signed; Xanana Gusmão signed), first enlargement since Cambodia 1999, application in 2011, in-principle agreement and observer status in 2022, Roadmap adopted in 2023, accession to the ASEAN Charter and the SEANWFZ Treaty before the summit; ARF in 2005. From memory: founding (8 Aug 1967, five countries) and the joining years of later members; Secretariat in Jakarta; English as working language (ASEAN Charter); two summits a year; 'about 680 million' people; ASEAN Day 8 August. Sources disagree on '13' or '14' years of effort (text says 'more than a decade'). Tetun: 'Mapa-Dalan', 'observadór', 'alargamentu', 'Zona Livre Kilat Nukleár'; the motto is kept in English as the official form.
- **g7+ and fragile states** (`tl.world.g7plus`, draft, 5 sources): Verified in searches: founded in Dili in April 2010 (10 April per Fundasaun Mahein) at the first International Dialogue meeting; secretariat in Dili; New Deal agreed in Busan, November 2011; about 20 members (Tatoli 2025; ODI once cited 19); g7+ meeting in Dili in 2025; Sierra Leone as a chair (South-South Galaxy, date not given); Dili Consensus (capacity4dev). From memory: the member examples (check the current list on g7plus.org), the five peacebuilding and statebuilding goals, Dili Consensus year 2013, Timor-Leste chairing in the early years. Wiki title 'G7+' not verified. Tetun: 'frajilidade', 'estadu frajil', 'previzivel', 'Konsensu Dili'.
- **Timor-Leste and the World Trade Organization (2024)** (`tl.world.wto`, draft, 8 sources): Checked in search results (WTO accession page, WTO/UN LDC news, Tatoli): working party 7 Dec 2016; MC13 approval and protocol signing in Abu Dhabi 26 Feb 2024 (with the Comoros); acceptance notified 31 Jul 2024; member on 30 Aug 2024 as the 166th member and 11th LDC since 1995; 'seven and a half years' of negotiations; acceptance of the Fisheries Subsidies Agreement (exact day not checked). Some sources wrongly give February 2024 as the membership date. The application date is unclear (one WTO item says 7 April 2015, others November 2016), so the text only says the process began in 2016; 'observer status in 2016' comes from a search summary — check. Phased tariff cuts 'over about ten years, especially non-agricultural goods such as electronics' come from the Taiwan-ASEAN Studies Center brief; the bound tariff schedule was not read. Philippines deal 25 Jan 2023 (DFA); EU talks finished March 2023 (Council of the EU document). Tetun terms: Organizasaun Mundiál Komérsiu, grupu servisu (working party), Protokolu Adezaun, nasaun ho dezenvolvimentu menus liu (LDC), Subsídiu ba Peska, Orden Timór-Leste, membru númeru 166.
- **Relations with Indonesia** (`tl.world.indonesia`, draft, 9 sources): Checked in search results: CTF set up Aug 2005, final report Per Memoriam ad Spem handed to both presidents 15 Jul 2008, finding of crimes against humanity (Wikipedia, Jakarta Post); trade Jan–Aug 2025 US$272.72m with Indonesian exports US$268.11m and imports from TL US$4.61m, '≈70% of products in Timorese markets' and the plan to update the traditional border crossings/regulated markets agreement (Antara 2025); agreement in principle on the last two land segments signed by Wiranto and Xanana in Jakarta on 22 Jul 2019 (Setkab/VOI); sea-border rounds Aug 2025 (Dili) to 1–3 Sep 2026 (Jakarta) (Tatoli); Prabowo signing the ASEAN admission declaration (Antara). Status of the open land segments differs: the ambassador (Dec 2025) says only Noel Besi–Citrana is left, while Tatoli (Sep 2026) lists Noel Besi–Citrana–Naktuka and Bidjael Sunan–Oben — the text only says talks on Noel Besi–Citrana continued. The 2005 provisional agreement is from memory/the border article. Numbers of Timorese students in Indonesia conflict (≈500 vs ≈5,000 in 2015), so no number is given; Indonesian scholarships are mentioned in 2015 reports. 'Indonesian TV, songs and newspapers are common' is general knowledge. Tetun: 'fatin hakat fronteira' (border crossing), 'relasaun babelun espesiál' (Konstituisaun Art. 8), 'Komisaun Verdade no Amizade (KVA)', 'ronda dahaat', 'Baikenu'.
- **Relations with Australia** (`tl.world.australia`, draft, 7 sources): Checked in search results: Australia is the largest development partner; total ODA 2025–26 budget estimate A$135.9m (2024–25 actual A$124.4m) and the Development Partnership Plan 2025–2030 (DFAT); PALM sectors, the 21 Dec 2024 Albanese–Xanana partnership of A$48.6m and the 10,000-worker target for 2027–28 (embassy, Tatoli); Dec 2013 raid on Collaery's office, cancelled passport of Witness K, Witness K's suspended sentence (2021) and dropped charges against Collaery (July 2022) (AP, Wikipedia). From memory, to check: the ICJ case on the seized documents and their return in 2015; WWII commandos 1942–43; de jure recognition in 1979; Cosgrove and INTERFET landing on 20 Sep 1999; ISF staying until early 2013; 'defence and police cooperate in training and maritime security' is general. Darwin's gas from Bayu-Undan: see tl.economy.petroleum (deliveries ended late 2023). Tetun: 'espionajen', 'Sasin K', 'pena suspensa', 'Parseria Mobilidade Traballu no Abilidade', 'uma ba ema katuas sira' (aged care), 'Forsa Estabilizasaun Internasionál'.
- **Relations with Portugal** (`tl.world.portugal`, draft, 7 sources): Checked in search results: ≈2,700 GNR/PSP/SEF members under UN mandates 1999–2013; Subagrupamento Bravo (2006) for public order and training of the PNTL rapid intervention unit; GNR cooperation since 2000 and 25 years marked in May 2025 (Lusa/Postal, Tatoli, Notícias ao Minuto); CAFE schools as the largest cooperation project (DN/Lusa); FOCO-UNTL Portuguese project with Camões (RTP); '≈80% of Portuguese public aid goes to education' (RTP item, year not seen — check). From memory/well known, to check: arrival c. 1515; Lifau 1702 and Dili 1769; governor's withdrawal to Ataúro (Aug 1975); UN listing Portugal as administering power; ICJ case filed 1991, judgment 30 June 1995; 5 May 1999 agreements in New York; Portuguese troops in the UN PKF from 2000; Portuguese judges and prosecutors in Timorese courts; diplomatic relations on 20 May 2002; CPLP membership 2002; Timorese with Portuguese nationality working in Portugal and the UK. Tetun: 'Revolusaun Kravu', 'podér administrante', 'Komunidade Nasaun sira ho Lian Portugés', 'Sentru Aprendizajen no Formasaun Eskolár', 'Eskola Portugeza Dili', 'unidade intervensaun lalais', 'tesi lia'.
- **Relations with China, Japan, the Pacific and others** (`tl.world.asia_pacific`, draft, 13 sources): Checked in search results: China first to establish relations (20 May 2002); comprehensive strategic partnership announced by Xi and Xanana in Hangzhou (Sept 2023); Ramos-Horta's July 2024 state visit and agreements (China Daily). JICA ≈US$480m over 20 years (Tatoli 2020); Hinode Bridge (Comoro Bridge III) grant up to ¥2.605bn (Wikipedia); Japanese flood-recovery help (2022) and road-machinery grant (Dec 2025) (Tatoli). EPS: TL included in 2009 (official start Oct 2009), ≈5,000 sent by 2023 (Devpolicy); 2024 remittances from South Korea ≈US$44.8m of US$244.8m (Central Bank figure quoted by Tatoli — check which Tatoli page). PIF observer since 2002 and MSG observer since 2010 (search summaries; one source also mentions a 2016 PIF step — unclear). Vietnam visa-exemption agreement June 2026 (US-ASEAN Business Council). Pope Francis 9–11 Sep 2024 and ≈600,000 at the Tasitolu Mass on 10 Sep (Vatican News, UPI). From memory, to check: Chinese grants for the Foreign Ministry building and the Presidential Palace, Chinese-built power stations; Japanese army engineers in UNMISET; NZ troops in INTERFET, the UNTAET south-west sector and the ISF; Filipino peacekeepers; first Timorese cardinal (Aug 2022); John Paul II's visit in Oct 1989; Timor-Leste on the UN SIDS list. Tetun: 'parseria estratéjika kompletu', 'Sistema Autorizasaun Empregu (EPS)', 'Grupu Melanéziu Ponta-Lansa', 'Forum Illa sira Pasífiku nian', 'nasaun illa ki'ik ne'ebé sei dezenvolve an', 'akordu izensaun vistu', 'Santa Sé'.
